Denotational semantics of general store and polymorphism
Jonathan Sterling ORCID:0000-0002-0585-5564, Daniel Gratzer ORCID:0000-0003-1944-0789, Lars Birkedal ORCID:0000-0003-1320-0098

Abstract
. We contribute the first denotational semantics of polymorphic dependent type theory extended by an equational theory for general (higher-order) reference types and recursive types, based on a combination of guarded recursion and impredicative polymorphism; because our model is based on recursively defined semantic worlds , it is compatible with polymorphism and relational reasoning about stateful abstract datatypes. We then extend our language with modal constructs for proof-relevant relational reasoning based on the logical relations as types principle, in which equivalences between imperative abstract datatypes can be established synthetically. What is new in relation to prior typed denotational models of higher-order store is that our Kripke worlds need not be syntactically definable, and are thus compatible with relational reasoning in the heap. Our work combines recent advances in the operational semantics of state with the purely denotational viewpoint of synthetic guarded domain theory .



1.
Introduction

The combination of parametric polymorphism and general reference types in
denotational semantics is notoriously difficult, though neither feature
presents any serious difficulties on its own.

(1)

Parametric polymorphism can be modelled in a
complete internal
category
à la Hyland
[
Hyl88
]
; for instance, the category of partial
equivalence relations has “large” products over the category of
assemblies, which contains the assembly of all partial equivalence
relations.

(2)

General reference types can be modeled via a Kripke world-indexed state
monad, where the worlds assign
syntactic
types to locations in the
heap, as in the possible worlds model of thunk storage by
Levy
[
Lev03
,
Lev02
]
.

The two techniques described above are not easily combined: in order to support
parametric reasoning in the presence of reference types, it is necessary at a
minimum for the Kripke worlds to assign
semantic
types to locations
rather than only syntactic types. But a semantic type should itself be a family
of (sets, predomains,
etc
.
) indexed in Kripke worlds; thus one attempts to
solve a domain equation of that defines a preorder
\mathbb{W}
of Kripke worlds simultaneously with
the category of functors
[
Rey81
,
Ole86
]
from
\mathbb{W}
to the category of predomains:

\mathbb{W}\cong{\mathsf{Loc}\rightharpoonup_{\mathit{fin.}}\mathsf{SemType}}\qquad\mathsf{SemType}\cong\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{W},\mathsf{Predomain}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{W},\mathsf{Predomain}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathsf{Predomain}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathsf{Predomain}$}\mathclose{}{]}}

There are already a few problems with the “domain equation” presented above.
For one, the collection of predomains is not itself a predomain, but it could
be replaced by the domain of finitary projections of some universal
domain
[
CGW94
]
. The more fundamental problem is
that it remains unclear how to interpret type connectives on
\mathsf{SemType}
;
for instance, Birkedal, Støvring, and
Thamsborg
[
BST10
, §5]
provide an explicit
counterexample to demonstrate that a naïve interpretation of the reference
type cannot coexist with recursive types in the presence of semantic worlds.

1.1.
Step-indexing and guarded domain theory

The difficulties outlined above have been side-stepped by the introduction of

step-indexing

[
Ahm04
,
AMRV07
]

and its semantic counterpart
metric/guarded domain
theory

[
AR87
,
RT92
,
BRS+11
]
, which proceed
by stratifying recursive definitions in their finite approximations. This
stratification was axiomatized by Birkedal
et al
.

[
BMSS11
]
in
synthetic
guarded domain theory
/ SGDT via the
later modality

; the standard model of SGDT is the topos
of trees
\mathscr{S}=\mathop{\mathbf{Pr}}{\omega}
where the later modality is defined like
so:

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\blacktriangleright}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\blacktriangleright}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\blacktriangleright}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\blacktriangleright}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}_{n}=\mathchoice{\textstyle\varprojlim_{k<n}}{\textstyle\varprojlim_{k<n}}{\scriptstyle\varprojlim_{k<n}}{\scriptscriptstyle\varprojlim_{k<n}}A_{k}\qquad\mathsf{next}_{A}^{n}x=k\mapsto x_{|k}

In the setting of SGDT, any recursive definition has a unique fixed
point if its recursive variables are guarded by an application of
{\blacktriangleright}
. In
categorical terms,
\mathscr{S}
is
algebraically compact
with respect
to
locally contractive
endofunctors. Letting
\mathbb{U}
be a type
universe in a model
\mathscr{S}
of synthetic guarded domain theory, it is
easy to solve the following approximate domain equation:

\mathbb{W}\cong\mathsf{Loc}\rightharpoonup_{\mathit{fin.}}{\color[rgb]{0.0117,0.2695,0.457}{\blacktriangleright}}\mathsf{SemType}\qquad\mathsf{SemType}\cong\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}

The preorder
\mathbb{W}
of Kripke worlds and the collection of semantic types

\mathsf{SemType}
both exist as objects of
\mathscr{S}
; it is even possible to
define a suitable connective
\mathsf{ref}:\hbox to93.75pt{\vbox to8.89pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 19.20836pt\lower-1.94444pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

.
We may also define an indexed type of heaps
\mathbb{H}:\mathbb{W}\to\mathbb{U}
; unfortunately it is quite unclear to define the

indexed state monad
for
\mathbb{H}
as a connective on
\mathsf{SemType}
.
For instance, the standard definition of the indexed state monad
[
PP02
,
Lev04
,
Pow11
]

runs into obvious size problems given that

\mathsf{SemType}\not\in\mathbb{U}
and thus
\mathbb{W}\not\in\mathbb{U}
:

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{T}\,{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{T}\,{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}w=\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}w^{\prime}\geq w}}{\textstyle\prod_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}w^{\prime}\geq w}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}w^{\prime}\geq w}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}w^{\prime}\geq w}}\mathbb{H}w^{\prime}\to\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}w^{\prime\prime}\geq w^{\prime}}}{\textstyle\sum_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}w^{\prime\prime}\geq w^{\prime}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}w^{\prime\prime}\geq w^{\prime}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}w^{\prime\prime}\geq w^{\prime}}}\mathbb{H}w^{\prime\prime}\times Aw^{\prime\prime}

Indeed, the definition above cannot be executed in the standard model

\mathscr{S}=\mathop{\mathbf{Pr}}{\omega}
when
\mathbb{U}
is the Hofmann–Streicher lifting of
a Grothendieck universe from
\mathbf{Set}
. This problem is side-stepped in

operational
models, where
\mathbb{U}
is replaced by a set of predicates on
syntactical expressions of the programming language being modeled and
\mathsf{T}

is defined by a guarded version of weakest preconditions as in the original
paper of Birkedal
et al
.

[
BMSS11
]
:

Operational methods worked in
\mathscr{S}

only because
the set of
predicates is a complete lattice: we may compute the join and intersection of
arbitrarily large families of predicates. This observation will ultimately form
the basis for our own non-operational solution; rather than giving up
on denotations, we will work in a
different
model of synthetic
guarded domain theory that contains an
impredicative universe

\mathbb{U}
,
i.e
.
one closed under universal and (thus) existential types à la
System F; then we can define the indexed state monad as follows
without encountering size problems:

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{T}\,{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{T}\,{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}w={\color[rgb]{0.0117,0.2695,0.457}\mathchoice{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{w^{\prime}\geq w}}{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{w^{\prime}\geq w}}{\scriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{321130}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{w^{\prime}\geq w}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{163842}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{w^{\prime}\geq w}}}\mathbb{H}w^{\prime}\to{\color[rgb]{0.0117,0.2695,0.457}\mathchoice{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\exists$}}}}}\displaylimits_{w^{\prime\prime}\geq This approach raises the question:
does there in fact exist a model of
synthetic guarded domain theory with an impredicative universe?
We answer in
the affirmative, constructing a model in presheaves on a well-founded order
internal to a
realizability topos

[
vO08
]
. Our model
of SGDT is thus an instance of the relative-topos-theoretic generalization of
synthetic guarded domain theory introduced by Palombi and Sterling
[
PS23
]
.

1.2.
This paper: impredicative guarded dependent type theory with reference types

Our denotational model of higher-order store was immediately suited for
generalization to dependent types; this scalability is one of many advantages
our abstract category-theoretic methods.
Justified by this model, we have defined Impredicative Guarded Dependent Type
Theory (
iGDTT
) and its extension with general reference types (
iGDTT
ref
)
and their equational theory.

iGDTT
ref
is the first language to soundly combine “full-spectrum”
dependently typed programming and equational reasoning with both higher-order
store and recursive types. The necessity of
semantics
for higher-order
store with dependent types is not hypothetical: both Idris 2 and Lean 4 are
dependently typed, higher-order functional programming
languages
[
Bra21
,
DMU21
]
that feature a Haskell-style

IO
monad with general
IORef
types
[
Jon01
]
, but
until now these features have had no semantics. In fact, our investigations
have revealed a potential problem in the
IO
monads of both Idris and
Lean: as higher-order store seems to be inherently impredicative, it may not
make sense to close multiple nested universes under the
IO

monad
[
Coq86
]
.

Many real-world examples require one to go
beyond
simple
equational reasoning; to this end, we defined an extension
iGDTT
{}^{\mathsf{ref}}_{\textsc{lrat}}

with constructs for synthetic, proof-relevant relational reasoning for data
abstraction and weak bisimulation based on the
logical relations as
types
(LRAT) principle of Sterling and Harper
[
SH21
]
, which axiomatizes a
generalization of the parametricity translation of dependent type
theory
[
BJP12
,
BM12
]
.

1.3.
Discussion of related work

1.3.1.
Operational semantics of higher-order store

The most thoroughly developed methods for giving semantics to higher-order
store are based on
operational semantics

[
Ahm04
]
;
accompanying the operational semantics of higher-order store is a wealth of
powerful program logics for modular reasoning about higher-order effectful and
even concurrent programs based on higher-order separation
logic
[
SB14
,
JKJ+18
,
BB18
]
.

We are motivated to pursue denotations for three reasons. First, denotational
methods are amenable to the use of general theorems from other mathematical
fields to solve difficult domain problems, whereas operational methods tend to
require one to solve every problem “by hand” without relying on standard
lemmas. Secondly, a good denotational semantics tends to simplify reasoning
about programs, as the exponents of the
DeepSpec
project have argued

[
XZH+19
]
. Finally and most profoundly, there is an emerging
need to program directly with
actual spaces
, as in differentiable and
probabilistic programming
languages
[
AP19
,
VKS19
]
.

Although operational and denotational semantics are different in both purpose
and technique, there is nonetheless a rich interplay between the two
traditions. First of all, the idea of step-indexing and its approximate
equational theory lies at the heart of our denotational semantics; second, the
wealth of results in operationally based models and program logics for
higher-order store suggest several areas for future work in our denotational
semantics. For instance, it would be interesting to rebase a program logic such
as Iris atop our synthetic denotational model; likewise, several perationally
based
works
[
ADR09
,
DNRB10
]

suggest many improvements to our notion of
semantic world
to support
richer kinds of correspondence between programs.

1.3.2.
Denotational semantics of state

There is a rich tradition of denotational semantics of state, ranging from a
single reference cell
[
Mog91
]
to first-order
store
[
PP02
]
and storage of
pointers
[
KLMS17
]
, and even higher-order store with
syntactic worlds
[
Lev04
]
. None of these approaches is compatible
with relational reasoning for polymorphic/abstract types, as they all
rely on the semantic heap being classified by syntactically definable types.

Untyped metric semantics and approximate locations

A somewhat different
untyped
approach to the denotational semantics of
higher-order store with recursive types and polymorphism was pioneered by
Birkedal, Støvring, and Thamsborg
[
BST10
]
in
which one uses metric/guarded domain theory to define a
universal domain

\mathsf{D}
, and then develops a model of System
\mathbf{F}_{\mu}^{\mathsf{ref}}
in predicates on

\mathsf{D}
. Because predicates form a complete lattice, it is possible to
interpret the state operations as we have discussed in

Section

1.1
. An important aspect of this class of models
is the presence of
approximate locations
, which
op. cit
.
have shown to
be non-optional.
1
1

1

Indeed, even in our own model the presence of
approximate locations can be detected using Thamsborg’s observation on the
correspondence between metric and ordinary domain
theory
[
Tha10
, Ch. 9]
.

Comparison

Our own model resembles a synthetic version of that of Birkedal, Støvring,
and Thamsborg
[
BST10
]
, but there are some
important differences. Our model is considerably more abstract and more general
than that of
op. cit
.
; whereas the cited work must solve a very complex metric
domain equation to construct a universal domain, we may use the realizability
topos generated by
any
partial combinatory algebra, and thus we do not
depend on any specific choice of universal domain. Moreover, we argue that
ours is a
typed
model: we depend only on the combination of guarded
recursion and an impredicative universe, and although the principle source of
such structures is realizability on untyped partial combinatory
algebras
[
LS02
]
, we do not depend on any of the details of
realizability. Because of the generality and abstractness of our model
construction, scaling up to full dependent type theory has offered no
resistance.

1.3.3.
Higher-order store in Impredicative Hoare Type Theory

Realizability models of impredicative type theory have been used before to give
a model of so-called Impredicative Hoare Type Theory (
iHTT
), an extension of
dependent type theory with a monadic “Hoare” type for stateful computations
with higher-order store
[
SBN11
]
. However, in
contrast to our monadic type in
iGDTT
ref
,
iHTT
does not support equational
reasoning about computations: all elements of a Hoare type are equated and one
can only reason via the types. Moreover, the Hoare type in
op. cit
.
only supports
untyped locations with substructural reference capabilities that change as
the heap evolves (the so-called “strong update”); thus non-Hoare types are not indexed in worlds.

1.3.4.
Linear dependent type theory

Another approach to the integration of state into dependent type theory is
contributed by Krishnaswami, Pradic, and
Benton
[
KPB15
]
, who develop an adjoint
linear–non-linear dependent type theory
LNL
d
with a realizability model in
partial equivalence relations. Like
iHTT
, the theory of
op. cit
.
uses
capabilities for references with strong update; unlike
iHTT
, the account of
store in
LNL
d
enjoys a rich equational theory. What is missing from
LNL
d

is any account of general recursion, which normally arises from higher-order
store via backpatching or Landin’s Knot; indeed,
LNL
d
carefully avoids the
general recursive aspects of higher-order store via linearity. One of the main
advances of our own paper over both
iHTT
and
LNL
d
is to model a
dependently typed equational theory for
full
store with general
recursion.

1.3.5.
Effectful dependent type theory

We have not attempted any non-trivial interaction between computational
effects and the dependent type structure of our language, in contrast to the
work of Pédrot and Tabareau
[
PT19
]
on dependent call-by-push-value: our

iGDTT
ref
language can be thought of as a purely functional programming
language extended with a monad for stateful programming with Haskell-style

IORef
s.

1.4.
Structure and contributions of this paper

Our contributions are as follows:

•

In
Section

2
we introduce
impredicative
guarded dependent type theory
(
iGDTT
) as a user-friendly metalanguage for
the denotational semantics of languages involving polymorphism, general
reference types and recursive types. In
Section

2.4
as a case
study, we construct a simple denotational model of Monadic System
\mathbf{F}_{\mu}^{\mathsf{ref}}
,
a language with general reference types, polymorphic types, and recursive
types in
iGDTT
. Finally in
Section

2.5
we describe our Coq
library for
iGDTT
in which we have formalized the higher-order state monad and
reference types.

•

In
Section

3
we describe
iGDTT
ref
, an
extension of
iGDTT
with general reference types and a monad for
higher-order store.
iGDTT
ref
is thus a full-spectrum dependently typed
programming language with support for higher-order effectful programming.
To illustrate the combination of higher-order store with dependent types,
we define and prove the correctness of an implementation of factorial
defined via
Landin’s knot
/ backpatching.

•

In
Section

4
we describe
iGDTT
{}^{\mathsf{ref}}_{\textsc{lrat}}
, an
extension of
iGDTT
ref
with constructs for synthetic proof-relevant
relational reasoning based on the
logical relations as types

principle
[
SH21
]
.
iGDTT
{}^{\mathsf{ref}}_{\textsc{lrat}}
can be used to
succinctly exhibit bisimulations between higher-order stateful computations
and abstract data types, which we demonstrate in two case studies involving
imperative counter implementations
(
Sections

4.3
and
4.4
).

•

In
Section

5
, we describe general results for
constructing models of
iGDTT
and
iGDTT
{}^{\mathsf{ref}}_{\textsc{lrat}}
, with concrete
instantiations given by a combination of realizability and internal
presheaves. The results of this section justify the consistency of

iGDTT
{}^{\mathsf{ref}}_{\textsc{lrat}}
as a language for relational reasoning about higher-order
stateful computations.

•

In
Section

6
we conclude with some reflections on directions for future work.

2.
Impredicative guarded dependent type theory

In this section we describe an extension of
guarded dependent type
theory
with an impredicative universe; guarded dependent type theory is a
dependently typed interface to synthetic guarded domain theory. The purpose of
this
impredicative guarded dependent type theory
(
iGDTT
) is to
serve as a metalanguage for the denotational semantics of programming languages
involving general reference types, just as ordinary guarded dependent type
theory can be used as a metalanguage for denotational semantics of programming
languages with recursive functions
[
PMB15
]
and
recursive types
[
MP16
]
.

2.1.
Universe structure: quantifiers and reflection

The core
iGDTT
language is modelled off of Martin-Löf type theory with a
pair of impredicative base universes
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\subseteq\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{P}\subseteq\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}\subseteq\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{P}\subseteq\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\in\mathbb{V}_{0}\in\mathbb{V}_{1}\in\ldots
, as in the version of the calculus of inductive
constructions with
impredicative Set
.
2
2

2

We mean that every
element of
\mathbb{P}
is also classified by
\mathbb{U}
, but we do not assert that

\mathbb{P}
is classified by
\mathbb{U}
.
The universes
\mathbb{U},\mathbb{V}_{i}
are
closed under dependent products, dependent sums, finite enumerations
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{]}}
,
inductive types (W-types), and extensional equality types with equality
reflection.
We assert that
\mathbb{P}
is both proof-irrelevant and univalent, and moreover
closed under extensional equality types. Proof-irrelevance means that for any

P:\mathbb{P}
and
p,q:P
we have
p=q
; univalence means that if

P\leftrightarrow Q
then
P=Q
.
3
3

3

This is also called

propositional extensionality
.

What makes
\mathbb{U},\mathbb{P}
impredicative is that we
assert an additional connective for
universal types
with abstraction,
application,
\beta
-, and
\eta
-laws.

impredicativity

\mathbb{S}\in\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{U},\mathbb{P}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{U},\mathbb{P}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{U},\mathbb{P}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{U},\mathbb{P}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
A:{\color[rgb]{0.0117,0.2695,0.457}\mathbb{V}_{i}}
x:A\vdash Bx:\mathbb{S}
\mathchoice{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{\scriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{321130}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{163842}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{Bx}:\mathbb{S}

The universal type automatically gives rise to an impredicative encoding of

existential
types. The naïve encoding
\mathchoice{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\exists$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\exists$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{\scriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{321130}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\exists$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{163842}{}{$\exists$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{Bx}\triangleq^{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}*}\mathchoice{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{C:\mathbb{S}}}{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{C:\mathbb{S}}}{\scriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{321130}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{C:\mathbb{S}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbo Bx\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle Bx\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle Bx\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Bx\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big)}{\big)}{\big)}{)}}}\to C

does not in fact have the correct universal property as its
\eta
-law holds
only up to parametricity, but we may use the method of
Awodey, Frey, and Speight
[
AFS18
]
to define a correct version of the existential
type. It will be simplest to do so in two steps: first define the

reflection

\color[rgb]{0.0117,0.2695,0.457}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}_{\mathbb{S}}:\mathbb{V}_{i}\to\mathbb{S}
, and then
apply this reflection to the dependent sum.

Theorem 1
.

The inclusion
\mathbb{S}
\mathbb{V}_{i}
has a left adjoint

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}_{\mathbb{S}}:\mathbb{V}_{i}\to\mathbb{S}
.

Proof 2.1
.

For reasons of space, we give only the definition of

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}_{\mathbb{S}}
. The reflection is constructed in two steps; first
we define the “wild” reflection

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}_{\mathbb{S}}:\mathbb{V}_{i}\to\mathbb{S}
by an impredicative
encoding, which is unfortunately too unconstrained to have the universal
property of the left adjoint:

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}_{\mathbb{S}}:\mathbb{V}_{i}\to\mathbb{S}

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}_{\mathbb{S}}\triangleq\mathchoice{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{C:\mathbb{S}}}{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{C:\mathbb{S}}}{\scriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{321130}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{C:\mathbb{S}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{163842}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{C:\mathbb{S}}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to C

We therefore constrain
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}_{\mathbb{S}}
by a naturality condition, encoded as a
structure
\mathsf{ok}_{A}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}_{\mathbb{S}}\to\mathbb{S}
defined using universal and equality
types like so:

\mathsf{ok}_{A}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}_{\mathbb{S}}\to\mathbb{S}

\mathsf{ok}_{A}\alpha\triangleq\mathchoice{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{C,D:\mathbb{S}}}{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{C,D:\mathbb{S}}}{\scriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{321130}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{C,D:\mathbb{S}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{163842}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{C,D:\mathbb{S}}}\mathchoice{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{f:C\to D}}{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{f:C\to D}}{\scriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{321130}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{f:C\to D}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{163842}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{f:C\to D}}\mathchoice{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{h:A\to C}}{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{h:A\to C}}{\scriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{321130}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{h:A\to C}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{163842}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{h:A\to C}}\alpha\,D\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f\circ h$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f\circ h$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f\circ h$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f\circ h$}\mathclose{}{)}}=f\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\alpha\,C\,h$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\alpha\,C\,h$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\alpha\,C\,h$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha\,C\,h$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\displaystyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\textstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}_{\mathbb{S}}:\mathbb{V}_{i}\to\mathbb{S}

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}_{\mathbb{S}}\triangleq\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{\alpha:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}_{\mathbb{S}}}}{\textstyle\sum_{\alpha:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}_{\mathbb{S}}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{\alpha:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}_{\mathbb{S}}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{\alpha:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}{\mathopen{}{\lvert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rvert}}_{\mathbb{S}}}}{\mathsf{ok}_{A}\alpha}

This completes the construction of the reflection.

We will write
\mathsf{pack}:A\to\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}{\mathopen{}{\lVert}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathclose{}{\rVert}}_{\mathbb{S}}
for the unit of the reflection; via
the universal property of the adjunction, maps into types classified by
\mathbb{S}
can be defined
by pattern matching,
e.g
.

\mathbf{let}\,\mathsf{pack}\,x=u\,\mathbf{in}\ v
; by virtue
of the constraint
\mathsf{ok}_{A}
, these destructuring expressions satisfy a desirable

\eta
-law.
With the reflection in hand, it is possible to give a correct encoding of the
existential type:

A:{\color[rgb]{0.0117,0.2695,0.457}\mathbb{V}_{i}}
x:A\vdash Bx:\mathbb{S}
\mathchoice{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\exists$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\exists$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{\scriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{321130}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\exists$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{163842}{}{$\exists$}}}}}\displaylimits_{x:A}}{Bx}\triangleq\mathchoice{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\lVert}{\big\lVert}{\big\lVert}{\lVert}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{x:A}}{\textstyle\sum_{x:A}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{x:A}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{x:A}}{Bx}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rVert}{\big\rVert}{\big\rVert}{\rVert}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\lVert}{\big\lVert}{\big\lVert}{\lVert}}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{x:A}}{\textstyle\sum_{x:A}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{x:A}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{x:A}}{Bx}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rVert}{\big\rVert}{\big\rVert}{\rVert}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\lVert}{\big\lVert}{\big\lVert}{\lVert}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{x:A}}{\textstyle\sum_{x:A}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{x:A}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{x:A}}{Bx}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rVert}{\big\rVert}{\big\rVert}{\rVert}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big\lVert}{\big\lVert}{\big\lVert}{\lVert}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{x:A}}{\textstyle\sum_{x:A}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{x:A}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{x:A}}{Bx}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big\rVert}{\big\rVert}{\big\rVert}{\rVert}}}_{\mathbb{S}}

The naïve impredicative encoding would have worked for
\mathbb{P}
because it
is already proof-irrelevant; but for uniformity, we present the reflections for

\mathbb{P},\mathbb{U}
simultaneously.

2.2.
The later modality and delayed substitutions

The remainder of
iGDTT
—the guarded fragment—is the same in prior
presentations
[
BGC+16
]
; to summarize, we have a type operator
{\blacktriangleright}

called the
later modality
equipped with a fixed-point operator. We
defer a proper exposition of guarded type theory to
Bizjak
et al
.

[
BGC+16
]
; intuitively, however, the type
{\blacktriangleright}A

contains elements of
A
which only become available one ‘step’ in the future.
We have chosen to present
{\blacktriangleright}
using the
delayed substitutions

\xi\leadsto\Xi
of
op. cit
.
:

\xi\leadsto\Xi
\Xi\vdash A\ \mathit{type}
{\blacktriangleright}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\xi$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\xi$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\xi$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\xi$}\mathclose{}{]}}.A\ \mathit{type}

\xi\leadsto\Xi
\Xi\vdash a:A
\mathsf{next}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\xi$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\xi$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\xi$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\xi$}\mathclose{}{]}}.{a}:{\blacktriangleright}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\xi$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\xi$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\xi$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\xi$}\mathclose{}{]}}.{A}

\cdot\leadsto\cdot

\xi\leadsto\Xi
a:{\blacktriangleright}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\xi$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\xi$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\xi$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\xi$}\mathclose{}{]}}.A
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\xi,x\leftarrow a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\xi,x\leftarrow a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\xi,x\leftarrow a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\xi,x\leftarrow a$}\mathclose{}{)}}\leadsto\Xi,x:A

The delayed substitution attached to the introduction and formation rules
allows an element of
{\blacktriangleright}A
to strip away the
{\blacktriangleright}
-modalities from a list
of terms, thereby rendering
{\blacktriangleright}
an applicative functor in the sense of
McBride and Paterson
[
MP08
]
:

f\circledast a\triangleq\mathsf{next}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle x\leftarrow f,y\leftarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle x\leftarrow f,y\leftarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x\leftarrow f,y\leftarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x\leftarrow f,y\leftarrow a$}\mathclose{}{]}}.x{y}

All universes
\mathbb{X}\in\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{U},\mathbb{P},\mathbb{V}_{i}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{U},\mathbb{P},\mathbb{V}_{i}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{U},\mathbb{P},\mathbb{V}_{i}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{U},\mathbb{P},\mathbb{V}_{i}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
are closed under
{\blacktriangleright}
.
We have equational laws governing both delayed substitutions and the

{\blacktriangleright}/\mathsf{next}
constructors; we will not belabor them here, referring instead to

op. cit
.
for a precise presentation. In the case of empty delayed substitutions

\xi=\cdot
, we will write
{\blacktriangleright}{A}
and
\mathsf{next}\,{a}
for
{\blacktriangleright}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\cdot$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\cdot$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}\mathclose{}{]}}.A

and
\mathsf{next}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\cdot$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\cdot$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}\mathclose{}{]}}.a
respectively. A guarded fixed point combinator
is included:

x:{\blacktriangleright}{A}\vdash fx:A
\mathsf{gfix}\,x.fx:A

x:{\blacktriangleright}{A}\vdash fx:A
\mathsf{gfix}\,x.fx=f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{next}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{gfix}\,x.fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{gfix}\,x.fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{gfix}\,x.fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{gfix}\,x.fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{next}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{gfix}\,x.fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{gfix}\,x.fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{gfix}\,x.fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{gfix}\,x.fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{next}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{gfix}\,x.fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{gfix}\,x.fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{gfix}\,x.fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{gfix}\,x.fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{next}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{gfix}\,x.fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{gfix}\,x.fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{gfix}\,x.fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{gfix}\,x.fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}:A

2.3.
Guarded domains and the lift monad

By construction, all types in
iGDTT
support a guarded fixed-point operator

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\blacktriangleright}A\to A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\blacktriangleright}A\to A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\blacktriangleright}A\to A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\blacktriangleright}A\to A$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to A
; without an algebra structure
{\blacktriangleright}A\to A
,
however, such a fixed point operator is insufficient to interpret general
recursion. We will define a
guarded domain
to be a type equipped with
exactly such an algebra structure below.

{defi}

We define a
guarded domain
to be a type
A
together with a
function
\vartheta_{A}:{\blacktriangleright}{A}\to A
,
i.e
.
an algebra for the later modality
viewed as an endofunctor.

Given a guarded domain
A
, we define the
delay map

\delta_{A}:A\to A
to be the composite
\vartheta_{A}\circ\mathsf{next}
. A guarded domain can be equipped with
a fixed point combinator
\mu_{A}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\to A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\to A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\to A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\to A$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to A
satisfying

\mu_{A}f=\delta_{A}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mu_{A}f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mu_{A}f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mu_{A}f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu_{A}f$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mu_{A}f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mu_{A}f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mu_{A}f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu_{A}f$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mu_{A}f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mu_{A}f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mu_{A}f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu_{A}f$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mu_{A}f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mu_{A}f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mu_{A}f$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mu_{A}f$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
. In particular, we define

\mu_{A}f\triangleq\mathsf{gfix}\,x.\,\vartheta_{A}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle z\leftarrow x$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle z\leftarrow x$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle z\leftarrow x$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle z\leftarrow x$}\mathclose{}{]}}.{fz}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle z\leftarrow x$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle z\leftarrow x$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle z\leftarrow x$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle z\leftarrow x$}\mathclose{}{]}}.{fz}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle z\leftarrow x$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle z\leftarrow x$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle z\leftarrow x$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle z\leftarrow x$}\mathclose{}{]}}.{fz}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle z\leftarrow x$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle z\leftarrow x$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle z\leftarrow x$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle z\leftarrow x$}\mathclose{}{]}}.{fz}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
.

{exa}

Each universe
\mathbb{X}\in\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{P},\mathbb{U},\mathbb{V}_{i}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{P},\mathbb{U},\mathbb{V}_{i}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{P},\mathbb{U},\mathbb{V}_{i}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{P},\mathbb{U},\mathbb{V}_{i}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
carries the structure of
a guarded domain, as we may define

\vartheta_{\mathbb{X}}A\triangleq{\blacktriangleright}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle X\leftarrow A$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle X\leftarrow A$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle X\leftarrow A$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X\leftarrow A$}\mathclose{}{]}}.X
.
Note that we have
\delta_{\mathbb{X}}A={\blacktriangleright}{A}
.

Because the universe is itself a guarded domain, the same fixed point
combinators can be used to interpret
both
recursive programs
and

recursive types as pointed out by Birkedal and
Møgelberg
[
BM13
]
; this is a significant improvement
over ordinary (synthetic) domain theory, where more complex notions of
algebraic compactness are required to lift recursion to the level of types.

Construction 2

(Guarded lift monad)
.

We will write
\mathbf{GDom}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{X}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{X}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{X}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{X}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
for the universe of guarded domains in a universe

\mathbb{X}
; the forgetful functor

\mathsf{J}:\hbox to114.53pt{\vbox to10pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 45.05559pt\lower-2.5pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

has a left adjoint

{\color[rgb]{0.0117,0.2695,0.457}\mathsf{L}}\dashv\mathsf{J}
that freely

a type to

\mathsf{L}\,{A}=A+{\blacktriangleright}\mathsf{L}\,{A}\qquad\qquad\vartheta_{\mathsf{L}\,{A}}\triangleq\mathsf{inr}

\mathsf{L}\,{A}=A+{\blacktriangleright}\mathsf{L}\,{A}
; we will write
\eta:A\to\mathsf{L}\,{A}
for the left
injection.

\mathsf{J}

.

2.4.
Case study: denotational semantics of Monadic System
\mathbf{F}_{\mu}^{\mathsf{ref}}

Already we have enough machinery to explore a simple possible worlds model of
System
\mathbf{F}_{\mu}^{\mathsf{ref}}
, a polymorphic language with general reference types. Even in this
simple case, the tension between polymorphism and general reference types is
evident and we require both the impredicative and guarded-recursive features of

iGDTT
to construct the
semantic worlds
that underly the model.
We emphasize that the entire construction takes place internally to
iGDTT
.

For the moment, we only sketch the interpretation of types in our model. We will
return to this point in
Section

5
, when we use a similar definition
of semantic worlds to construct a model for a version of full
iGDTT
extended
with general references.

2.4.1.

\mathsf{World}:\hbox to40.89pt{\vbox to8.33pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 5.99307pt\lower-1.5pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

; thus we have

{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathcal{T}=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{World}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\blacktriangleright}{\mathcal{T}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\blacktriangleright}{\mathcal{T}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\blacktriangleright}{\mathcal{T}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\blacktriangleright}{\mathcal{T}}$}\mathclose{}{)}},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{World}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\blacktriangleright}{\mathcal{T}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\blacktriangleright}{\mathcal{T}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\blacktriangleright}{\mathcal{T}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\blacktriangleright}{\mathcal{T}}$}\mathclose{}{)}},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{World}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\blacktriangleright}{\mathcal{T}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\blacktriangleright}{\mathcal{T}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\blacktriangleright}{\mathcal{T}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\blacktriangleright}{\mathcal{T}}$}\mathclose{}{)}},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{World}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\blacktriangleright}{\mathcal{T}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\blacktriangleright}{\mathcal{T}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\blacktriangleright}{\mathcal{T}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\blacktriangleright}{\mathcal{T}}$}\mathclose{}{)}},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}}
. Finally we
define
\mathbb{W}
to be the preorder
\mathsf{World}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\blacktriangleright}{\mathcal{T}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\blacktriangleright}{\mathcal{T}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\blacktriangleright}{\mathcal{T}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\blacktriangleright}{\mathcal{T}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
. Thus
we have solved the domain equation
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathbb{W}=\mathbb{N}\rightharpoonup_{\mathit{fin.}}{\blacktriangleright}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}}
and we have
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathcal{T}=\mathsf{ob}_{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}}}
.
The object
\mathcal{T}
can be seen to be a guarded domain, setting

\vartheta_{\mathcal{T}}A\triangleq\lambda w.{\blacktriangleright}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle X\leftarrow A$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle X\leftarrow A$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle X\leftarrow A$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X\leftarrow A$}\mathclose{}{]}}.Xw
.

2.4.2.
Higher-order state monad and reference types

We now define a strong monad
\mathsf{T}
on
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}

below:

.

2.4.3.
Synthetic model of Monadic System
\mathbf{F}_{\mu}^{\mathsf{ref}}

We may now implement a denotational semantics of Monadic System
\mathbf{F}_{\mu}^{\mathsf{ref}}
in

iGDTT
; the direct-style call-by-value version of System
\mathbf{F}_{\mu}^{\mathsf{ref}}
can then be
interpreted separately à la Moggi in the standard way. We specify the domains
of interpretation for each form of judgment below:

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Xi\vdash$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\Xi\vdash$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Xi\vdash$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Xi\vdash$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}:\mathbb{V}\hskip \mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{V}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{V}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{V}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{V}$}\mathclose{}{]}}

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}
respectively:

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\cdot\vdash$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\cdot\vdash$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\cdot\vdash$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot\vdash$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}\triangleq 1

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Xi,\alpha\vdash$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\Xi,\alpha\vdash$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Xi,\alpha\vdash$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Xi,\alpha\vdash$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}\triangleq\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Xi\vdash$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\Xi\vdash$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Xi\vdash$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Xi\vdash$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}\times\mathcal{T}

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Xi\mid\cdot\vdash$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\Xi\mid\cdot\vdash$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Xi\mid\cdot\vdash$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Xi\mid\cdot\vdash$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}\xi\triangleq 1

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Xi\mid\Gamma,x:\tau\vdash$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\Xi\mid\Gamma,x:\tau\vdash$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Xi\mid\Gamma,x:\tau\vdash$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Xi\mid\Gamma,x:\tau\vdash$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}\xi\triangleq\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}\xi\times\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Xi\vdash\tau\ \mathit{type}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\Xi\vdash\tau\ \mathit{type}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Xi\vdash\tau\ \mathit{type}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Xi\vdash\tau\ \mathit{type}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}\xi

Individual type connectives are interpreted below:

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Xi\vdash\forall\alpha.\tau\ \mathit{type}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\Xi\vdash\forall\alpha.\tau\ Function and product types are interpreted using the cartesian closure of

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}
; note that functor categories with relatively large
domain are not typically cartesian closed, but in our case the impredicativity
of
\mathbb{U}\in\mathbb{V}
ensures cartesian closure.
Next we interpret the state operations as families of natural transformations
in
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{]}}
:

In the interpretation of the
\mathsf{new}_{\tau}\,
operator, we have assumed a
deterministic “allocator”
\mathsf{fresh}:\mathsf{ob}_{\mathbb{W}}\to\mathbb{N}
that
chooses an unused address in a given world. Note that the naturality conditions
of the interpretation do not depend in any way on the behavior of

\mathsf{fresh}
.

2.4.4.
Abstract steps in the equational theory of higher-order store

Notice that our interpretation of the getter must invoke the
{\blacktriangleright}
-algebra
structure on
\mathsf{T}\,{A}
as the heap associates to each location
i
an
element of
\vartheta_{\mathcal{T}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle wi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle wi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle wi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle wi$}\mathclose{}{)}}
. For this reason, equations
governing
reads
to the heap do not hold on the nose but rather hold up
to an abstract “step”.

For example, given
l:\mathsf{ref}\,{A}
we might expect the equation

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{set}_{A}\,l\,a;\mathsf{get}_{A}l$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{set}_{A}\,l\,a;\mathsf{get}_{A}l$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{set}_{A}\,l\,a;\mathsf{get}_{A}l$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{set}_{A}\,l\,a;\mathsf{get}_{A}l$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{set}_{A}\,l\,a;\mathsf{ret}\,{a}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{set}_{A}\,l\,a;\mathsf{ret}\,{a}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{set}_{A}\,l\,a;\mathsf{ret}\,{a}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{set}_{A}\,l\,a;\mathsf{ret}\,{a}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}
to hold, but
the call to
\vartheta
in the former denotation will prevent them from
agreeing in our model. This behavior is familiar already from the
work of Escardó
[
Esc99
]
and Paviotti
et al
.

[
PMB15
]
on denotational semantics of
general recursion in guarded/metric domain theory: the guarded interpretation of recursion forces a more
intensional notion of equality that counts abstract steps. Thus in order to
properly formulate the equations that
do
hold, we must account for these
abstract steps. To this end, we extend Monadic System
\mathbf{F}_{\mu}^{\mathsf{ref}}
by a new
primitive effect
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathsf{step}:\mathsf{T}\,{1}}
that takes an abstract step:

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash{\mathsf{step}:\mathsf{T}\,{1}}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash{\mathsf{step}:\mathsf{T}\,{1}}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash{\mathsf{step}:\mathsf{T}\,{1}}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash{\mathsf{step}:\mathsf{T}\,{1}}$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}\xi w\gamma w^{\prime},\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle h,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle h,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle w^{\prime},\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle
