h,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle h,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle h,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

With the above in hand, we may state and prove the expected equations for state:

Theorem 4
.

Our denotational semantics of Monadic System
\mathbf{F}_{\mu}^{\mathsf{ref}}
validates the following equations:

\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash{l:\mathsf{ref}\,{\tau}}
\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash{u:\tau}
\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash{\mathsf{set}_{\tau}\,l\,u;\mathsf{get}_{\tau}l\equiv{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathsf{step}};\mathsf{set}_{\tau}\,l\,u;\mathsf{ret}\,{u}:\mathsf{T}\,{\tau}}

\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash{l:\mathsf{ref}\,{\tau}}
\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\tau}l;\mathsf{set}_{\tau}\,l\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\tau}l;\mathsf{set}_{\tau}\,l\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\tau}l;\mathsf{set}_{\tau}\,l\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\tau}l;\mathsf{set}_{\tau}\,l\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}\equiv{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathsf{step}}:\mathsf{T}\,1}

\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash{u,v:\tau}
\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x\leftarrow\mathsf{new}_{\tau}\,u;\mathsf{set}_{\tau}\,x\,v;\mathsf{ret}\,{x}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x\leftarrow\mathsf{new}_{\tau}\,u;\mathsf{set}_{\tau}\,x\,v;\mathsf{ret}\,{x}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x\leftarrow\mathsf{new}_{\tau}\,u;\mathsf{set}_{\tau}\,x\,v;\mathsf{ret}\,{x}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x\leftarrow\mathsf{new}_{\tau}\,u;\mathsf{set}_{\tau}\,x\,v;\mathsf{ret}\,{x}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\equiv\mathsf{new}_{A}\,v:\mathsf{T}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{ref}\,{\tau}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{ref}\,{\tau}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{ref}\,{\tau}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{ref}\,{\tau}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}

\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash{l:\mathsf{ref}\,{\tau}}
\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash{u,v:\tau}
\Xi\mid\Gamma\vdash{\mathsf{set}_{\tau}\,l\,u;\mathsf{set}_{\tau}\,l\,v\equiv\mathsf{set}_{\tau}\,l\,v:\mathsf{T}\,1}

Proof 2.2
.

Immediate upon unfolding the denotational semantics of the given equations.

2.5.
Formal case study: presheaf model of higher-order store in Coq

Although the semantics of
iGDTT
are admittedly quite sophisticated (see

Section

5
), one of its advantages is that it is quite
straightforward to extend existing proof assistants such as Coq
[
Coq16
]
and Agda
[
Nor09
]
with
the axioms of
iGDTT
. Thus a practitioner of programming language semantics
can define “naïve” synthetic models of effects such as higher-order store
in a proof assistant without needing to know the category theory that was
required to invent and justify
iGDTT
.

To validate the use of
iGDTT
for formal semantics of programming languages,
we have postulated the axioms of
iGDTT
in a Coq library and used it to
formalize the construction of the indexed state monad
\mathsf{T}
defined in

Section

2.4.2
as well as the reference type connective. In our
formalization, we decompose
\mathsf{T}
as the horizontal composition of several
adjunctions and thus obtain the monad laws for free by taking
\mathsf{T}
to be
the monad of the composite adjunction.

2.5.1.
Axiomatizing the impredicative universe

We take Coq’s
Set
universe to be the impredicative universe
\mathbb{U}
of
iGDTT
;
it is possible to execute Coq with the flag
-impredicative-set
, but we
have found it simpler to implement the impredicativity by means of a local pragma
as follows:

#[bypass_check(universes = yes)]

Definition
All {A : Type} {B : A -> Set} : Set :=

forall x : A, B x.

For
iGDTT
’s predicative universes
\mathbb{V}_{i}
we use Coq’s predicative
universes
Type@{i}
. We formalize the left adjoint to the inclusion

\mbox{Set}\hookrightarrow\mbox{Type@\lx@text@lbrace i\lx@text@rbrace}
, verifying its universal property
using the argument of Awodey, Frey, and Speight
[
AFS18
]
.

2.5.2.
Axiomatizing guarded recursion

Next we axiomatize the later modality in Coq. Coq’s implicit universe
polymorphism ensures that the following axioms land not only in
Type

but in
Set
as well:

Axiom
later : Type -> Type.

Notation
"
{\blacktriangleright}
A" := (later A) (at level 60).

Axiom
next : forall A, A ->
{\blacktriangleright}
A.

Axiom
loeb : forall A (f :
{\blacktriangleright}
A -> A), A.

Axiom
loeb_unfold : forall A (f :
{\blacktriangleright}
A -> A), loeb f = f (next (loeb f)).

We do not include the general delayed substitutions in our axiomatization,
because they are too difficult to formalize; for our case study, the following
special case of delayed substitutions suffices:

Axiom
dlater :
{\blacktriangleright}
Type -> Type.

Axiom
dlater_next_eq : forall A,
{\blacktriangleright}
A = dlater (next A).

2.5.3.
Formalizing guarded interaction trees

Building on the above and several other axioms of guarded recursion that we
omit for lack of room, we formalize a notion of guarded algebraic effect based
on
containers

[
AAG05
]
. Given a container

\mathbb{E}=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle S:\mathbb{U},P:S\to\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle S:\mathbb{U},P:S\to\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle S:\mathbb{U},P:S\to\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle S:\mathbb{U},P:S\to\mathbb{U}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
, we may view each
s:S
as
the name of an effect operation for which
Ps
is the continuation arity.
Writing
\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{E}}X=\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{s:S}}{\textstyle\sum_{s:S}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{s:S}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{s:S}}\mathchoice{\textstyle\prod_{p:Ps}}{\textstyle\prod_{p:Ps}}{\scriptstyle\prod_{p:Ps}}{\scriptscriptstyle\prod_{p:Ps}}X
for the
polynomial endofunctor presented by
\mathbb{E}
, we may consider for each
A

the free
\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{E}}
algebra generated by
A
and the
(co)-free
\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{E}}
coalgebra generated by
A
; the former
is the inductive type of finite (terminating) computations of a value of type

A
that use the effects specified by
\mathbb{E}
, whereas the latter is the

coinductive
type of possibly infinite computations. The latter are
referred to as
interaction trees
by Xia
et al
.

[
XZH+19
]
,
who have propelled a resurgence in their use for reasoning about code in
first-order languages via denotational semantics.

We formalize a
guarded
version of interaction trees, obtained by solving
the guarded domain equation
X=A+\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{E}}{\blacktriangleright}{X}
using
loeb
in
Set
.

Record
Container := {op : Set; bdry : op -> Set}.

Inductive
ITree_F (
\mathbb{E}
: Container) A T : Set) : Set :=

| Ret (r : A)

| Do (e :
\mathbb{E}
) (k : bdry e -> T).

Definition
ITree (
\mathbb{E}
: Container) (A : Set) : Set :=

loeb (fun T => ITree_F (dlater T)).

We develop a free-forgetful adjunction for each container
\mathbb{E}
; in
particular, we prove that
ITree
\mathbb{E}
A
is the free

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{E}}\circ{\blacktriangleright}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{E}}\circ{\blacktriangleright}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{E}}\circ{\blacktriangleright}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{E}}\circ{\blacktriangleright}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
-algebra generated by
A
. The
reason for developing guarded interaction trees is that there is a particular
container
\mathbb{E}
for which
ITree
\mathbb{E}
is the
guarded
lift monad

\mathsf{L}
of Paviotti, Møgelberg, and
Birkedal
[
PMB15
]
, which we have discussed in

Section

2.3
. Nonetheless, the extra generality allows us to
modularly add other effects such as failure or printing,
etc
.

2.5.4.
Recursively defined worlds, presheaves, and the state monad

We start by defining the category of finite maps of elements of a given type:

Definition
world : Type -> Category.type := ...

Then we define the collections of semantic worlds and semantic types by solving a
guarded domain equation, where we write
SET
for the category induced by
the impredicative
Set
universe.

Definition

\mathcal{F}
(T : Type) : Type := [world T, SET].

Definition

\mathcal{T}
: Type := loeb (fun T =>
\mathcal{F}
(dlater T)).

Definition

\mathbb{W}
: Category.type := world
\mathcal{T}
.

Definition
heap (w :
\mathbb{W}
) : Type := ...

Definition

\mathbb{H}
: Type := {w :
\mathbb{W}
& heap w}.

We have also formalized the reference type connective as a map
ref :

\mathcal{T}
->
\mathcal{T}
, exactly as in the informal presentation of

Section

2.4.2
. Our formalization of the state monad differs from our
informal presentation in two ways: firstly we have generalized over an
arbitrary container
\mathbb{E}
specifying computational effects (
e.g
.
printing, failure,
etc
.
), and secondly we found it simpler to decompose it into
the following sequence of simpler adjunctions, whose definition involves the
impredicative
All
quantifier that we axiomatized earlier:

\mathchoice{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big[}{\big[}{\big[}{[}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{W}^{\circ},\mbox{Alg $\mathbb{E}$}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big]}{\big]}{\big]}{]}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big[}{\big[}{\big[}{[}}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{W}^{\circ},\mbox{Alg $\mathbb{E}$}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big]}{\big]}{\big]}{]}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big[}{\big[}{\big[}{[}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{W}^{\circ},\mbox{Alg $\mathbb{E}$}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big]}{\big]}{\big]}{]}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big[}{\big[}{\big[}{[}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{W}^{\circ},\mbox{Alg $\mathbb{E}$}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big]}{\big]}{\big]}{]}}}
\mathchoice{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big[}{\big[}{\big[}{[}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{W}^{\circ},\mbox{Set}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big]}{\big]}{\big]}{]}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big[}{\big[}{\big[}{[}}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{W}^{\circ},\mbox{Set}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big]}{\big]}{\big]}{]}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big[}{\big[}{\big[}{[}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{W}^{\circ},\mbox{Set}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big]}{\big]}{\big]}{]}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big[}{\big[}{\big[}{[}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{W}^{\circ},\mbox{Set}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big]}{\big]}{\big]}{]}}}
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{H},\mbox{Set}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{H},\mbox{Set}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{H},\mbox{Set}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{H},\mbox{Set}$}\mathclose{}{]}}
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{W},\mbox{Set}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{W},\mbox{Set}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mbox{Set}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mbox{Set}$}\mathclose{}{]}}
\mathchoice{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big[}{\big[}{\big[}{[}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{W}^{\circ},\mbox{Forget}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big]}{\big]}{\big]}{]}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big[}{\big[}{\big[}{[}}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{W}^{\circ},\mbox{Forget}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big]}{\big]}{\big]}{]}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big[}{\big[}{\big[}{[}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{W}^{\circ},\mbox{Forget}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big]}{\big]}{\big]}{]}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big[}{\big[}{\big[}{[}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{W}^{\circ},\mbox{Forget}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big]}{\big]}{\big]}{]}}}
\mathchoice{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big[}{\big[}{\big[}{[}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{W}^{\circ},\mbox{ITree $\mathbb{E}$}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big]}{\big]}{\big]}{]}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big[}{\big[}{\big[}{[}}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{W}^{\circ},\mbox{ITree $\mathbb{E}$}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big]}{\big]}{\big]}{]}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big[}{\big[}{\big[}{[}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{W}^{\circ},\mbox{ITree $\mathbb{E}$}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big]}{\big]}{\big]}{]}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big[}{\big[}{\big[}{[}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{W}^{\circ},\mbox{ITree $\mathbb{E}$}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big]}{\big]}{\big]}{]}}}
\bot
\mathchoice{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}}\displaylimits_{\mbox{heap}}}{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}}\displaylimits_{\mbox{heap}}}{\scriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{321130}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}}\displaylimits_{\mbox{heap}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{163842}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}}\displaylimits_{\mbox{heap}}}
\mathchoice{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\exists$}}}}}\displaylimits_{\mbox{heap}}}{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\exists$}}}}}\displaylimits_{\mbox{heap}}}{\scriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{321130}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\exists$}}}}}\displaylimits_{\mbox{heap}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{163842}{}{$\exists$}}}}}\displaylimits_{\mbox{heap}}}
\bot
\mathchoice{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{\mbox{heap}}}{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{\mbox{heap}}}{\scriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{321130}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{\mbox{heap}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{163842}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits_{\mbox{heap}}}
\mathchoice{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}}\displaylimits_{\mbox{heap}}}{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}}\displaylimits_{\mbox{heap}}}{\scriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{321130}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}}\displaylimits_{\mbox{heap}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{163842}{}{$\operatorname{\Delta}$}}}}}\displaylimits_{\mbox{heap}}}
\bot

We did not formalize the state operations,
but there is no obstacle to doing so.

3.
Impredicative guarded dependent type theory with reference types

In this section, we detail an extension
iGDTT
ref
of
iGDTT
with
general reference types and a state monad.
iGDTT
ref
can serve as both an
effectful higher-order programming language in its own right,
and
as a
dependently typed metalanguage for the denotational semantics of other
programming languages involving higher-order store. Later on we will extend

iGDTT
ref
with relational constructs to enable the
verification
of
stateful programs inside the type theory. Denotational semantics for these
extensions of
iGDTT
are discussed in
Section

5
.

3.1.
Adding reference types to
iGDTT

In this section, we extend the
iGDTT
language with the following constructs:

(1)

a monad
\mathsf{T}:\mathbb{U}\to\mathbb{U}
,

(2)

a later algebra structure
\vartheta_{\mathsf{T}\,{A}}:{\blacktriangleright}{\mathsf{T}\,{A}}\to\mathsf{T}\,{A}
parameterized in
A:\mathbb{U}
such that:

u:\mathsf{T}\,{A}
x:A\vdash vx:\mathsf{T}\,{B}
\delta_{\mathsf{T}\,{B}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x\leftarrow u;vx$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x\leftarrow u;vx$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x\leftarrow u;vx$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x\leftarrow We will write
\mathsf{step}:\mathsf{T}\,{1}
for the generic effect
\delta_{\mathsf{T}\,{1}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{ret}\,{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{ret}\,{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{ret}\,{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{ret}\,{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
; the rule above ensures that
\mathsf{step}
commutes with all operations in the monad.

(3)

a reference type connective closed under the following rules:

A:\mathbb{U}
\mathsf{ref}\,{A}:\mathbb{U}

3.2.
Programming with higher-order store and dependent types

3.2.1.
Type dependency in the heap

Because the universe
\mathbb{U}
is closed under dependent types (including
equality types,
etc
.
), there is no obstacle to storing elements of dependent
types in the heap. For instance, it is easy to allocate a reference that
can only hold even integers; as an example, we consider a program that increments an
even integer in place, abbreviating
T\triangleq\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle z:\mathbb{Z}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{isEven}\,z$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle z:\mathbb{Z}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{isEven}\,z$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle z:\mathbb{Z}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{isEven}\,z$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle z:\mathbb{Z}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{isEven}\,z$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
:

M:\mathsf{ref}\,{T}\to\mathsf{T}\,{1}

M\triangleq\lambda l.x\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{T}l;\mathsf{set}_{T}\,l\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x+2$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x+2$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x+2$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x+2$}\mathclose{}{)}}

involving

via Landin’s knot, writing
\bot:\mathsf{L}\,{\alpha}
for the divergent element
\mu x.x
.

We can use the backpatching knot to give a (contrived) computation of the factorial:

\mathsf{fact}^{\prime}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{N}\to\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{N}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{N}\to\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{N}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{N}\to\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{N}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{N}\to\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{N}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\mathbb{N}\to\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{N}}

\mathsf{fact}^{\prime}\,f\,0\triangleq\mathsf{ret}\,{1}

\mathsf{fact}^{\prime}\,f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}\triangleq m\leftarrow f\,n;\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}\times m$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}\times m$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}\times m$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}\times m$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\mathsf{fact}:\mathbb{N}\to\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{N}}

\mathsf{fact}\triangleq\mathsf{knot}\,\mathbb{N}\,\mathsf{fact}^{\prime}

Lemma 5
.

Entirely inside of
iGDTT
ref
we may prove the
following correctness lemma given a reference implementation
\mathsf{goodFact}:\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}
:

.

Remark 6
.

Note that we do not have

\mathsf{fact}\,n=\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{goodFact}\,n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{goodFact}\,n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{goodFact}\,n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{goodFact}\,n$}\mathclose{}{)}}
; to understand why this is the
case, there are two things to take note of. First, the abstract steps
taken by each access to the heap are explicitly tracked by the equational
theory of
iGDTT
ref
as an instance of the
\mathsf{step}
effect. Secondly, even
though the allocations carried out by
\mathsf{patch}
are no longer active upon
return, the equational theory of
iGDTT
ref
cannot “garbage collect” them.

4.
Logical relations as types for recursion and higher-order store

Like any dependent type theory,
iGDTT
ref
is equipped with a “built-in”
notion of equality, but it is too fine to serve as a basis for reasoning about
the equivalence of effectful programs. For instance, we have already seen that
even the most basic programs governing reading and writing to a reference may
differ in the number of steps they take. Accordingly, we will construct a coarser
notion of equivalence between programs in the form of a logical relation over

iGDTT
ref
.

In particular, we discuss an extension of the axioms of
iGDTT
ref
which
supports proof-relevant binary relational reasoning in the sense of
Sterling and Harpers’s
logical relations as types
(LRAT)
principle
[
SH21
]
.
4
4

4

The LRAT language is also referred to in other contexts as

synthetic Tait computability

[
Ste21
]
.
The
result is
iGDTT
{}^{\mathsf{ref}}_{\textsc{lrat}}
, a new dependent type theory with lightweight
features for verifying the equivalence of effectful computations. In addition
to capturing the traditional “method of candidates” reasoning,

iGDTT
{}^{\mathsf{ref}}_{\textsc{lrat}}
includes a notion of weak
bisimulation
[
MP16
,
Pav16
]
. The inclusion of
weak bisimulation is crucial; without it, only programs that read from the heap
in lockstep could ever be related.

Finally, we show how our synthetic approach simplifies two standard examples
from Birkedal, Støvring, and Thamsborg
[
BST10
]
concerning equivalent
implementations of imperative counters.

4.1.
Logical relations as types: a synthetic approach to relational reasoning

The
logical relations as types
principle states that logical
relations can be treated
synthetically
as ordinary types in the presence
of certain modal operators that exist in categories of logical relations. Thus
LRAT is an abstraction of logical relations that avoids the bureaucracy of
analytic approaches; the “work” that goes into conventional logical relations
arguments has not disappeared, but has been refactored into general results of
category theory that are much simpler to establish directly than almost any of
their practical consequences in programming languages.

We execute the LRAT extension
iGDTT
{}^{\mathsf{ref}}_{\textsc{lrat}}
of
iGDTT
ref
by
postulating three propositions
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}:\mathbb{P}
representing the
left- and right-hand sides of a correspondence, and the disjoint union of both
sides; the idea is that these propositions will generate modal operators that
allow us to think of
any
type as a correspondence, and then project out
different aspects of the correspondence.

\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}:\mathbb{P}

\_:\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}
\_:\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}
\_:\bot

Above we have imposed a rule to make
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}
disjoint. We additionally
add rules to make
\boldsymbol{\Phi}
behave as the
coproduct

\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}+\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}
, with a
splitting rule not only for propositions but also for types.

\_:\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\vdash a_{L}:A
\_:\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\vdash a_{R}:A
\_:\boldsymbol{\Phi}\vdash\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow a_{L},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow a_{R}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow a_{L},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow a_{R}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow a_{L},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow a_{R}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow a_{L},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow a_{R}$}\mathclose{}{]}}:A

\_:\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\vdash a_{L}:A
\_:\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\vdash a_{R}:A
\_:\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\vdash\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow a_{L},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow a_{R}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow a_{L},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow a_{R}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow a_{L},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow a_{R}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow a_{L},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow a_{R}$}\mathclose{}{]}}=a_{L}:A

\_:\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\vdash a=b:A
\_:\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\vdash a=b:A
\_:\boldsymbol{\Phi}\vdash a=b:A

Remark 7
.

In other applications of LRAT such as those of
Sterling
et al
.

[
SA21
,
SH22
]
, case splits
were included for arbitrary
\phi\lor\psi
. This is possible in the internal language of a topos, but

not
in the
iGDTT
where the universe of propositions is meant to lie
within
\mathbb{V}_{i}
, which will be interpreted by a quasitopos rather than a
true topos. Roughly the issue is that in a quasitopos
\mathscr{E}
, the union of
two regular subobjects need not be regular unless
\mathscr{E}
is
quasiadhesive
[
JLS07
]
.

The modalities that we consider in this paper are all
left exact,
idempotent, monadic
modalities in the sense of
Rijke, Shulman, and Spitters
[
RSS20
]
. Left exactness means that they preserve
finite limits, and idempotence means that the multiplication map
T^{2}\to T
is
a natural isomorphism. In this section, let
T
be an arbitrary left exact
idempotent monad.

{defi}

[Modal types]
We will refer to a type
A
as

T
-modal
when the unit map
\eta_{A}:\hbox to39.15pt{\vbox to7.16pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 3.75pt\lower-0.33063pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{nota}

[Modal elimination]
Idempotent monadic modalities enjoy a universal property: for any
T
-modal
type
C
, the function
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle TA\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle TA\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle TA\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle TA\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle A\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle A\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A\to C$}\mathclose{}{)}}
induced by
precomposition with the unit is an isomorphism. We reflect this in our
notation as follows:

C\text{ is $T$-modal}
u:TA
x:A\vdash fx:C
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x\leftarrow u;fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x\leftarrow u;fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x\leftarrow u;fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x\leftarrow u;fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}:C

\ldots
a:A
x:A\vdash fx:C
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x\leftarrow\eta a;fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x\leftarrow\eta a;fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x\leftarrow\eta a;fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x\leftarrow\eta a;fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}=fa:C

C\text{ is $T$-modal}
x:TA\vdash fx,gx:C
x:A\vdash f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\eta x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\eta x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\eta x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\eta x$}\mathclose{}{)}}=g\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\eta x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\eta x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\eta x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\eta x$}\mathclose{}{)}}:C
u:TA
fu=gu:C

Let
\phi:\mathbb{P}
be a proposition.

{defi}

A type
A
is called
\phi
-transparent
when the constant map

A
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\phi\to A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\phi\to A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phi\to A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phi\to A$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is an isomorphism; on the other hand,
A
is
called
\phi
-sealed
when the projection map
\phi\times A
\phi
is an isomorphism. Equivalently,
A
is
\phi
-sealed exactly when

\phi
implies that
A
is a singleton; we will write
\star:A
for the
unique element of any
\top
-sealed type
A
.

Intuitively, a
\phi
-transparent type is one that “thinks”
\phi
is true;
conversely, a
\phi
-sealed type is one that “thinks”
\phi
is false and
thus contracts to a point under
\phi
.
The
\phi
-transparent and
\phi
-sealed types are each governed by modalities,
referred to as
open
and
closed
respectively.

{defi}

[Open modality]
We will write
\phi\Rightarrow{A}
for the
implicit
function space

\phi\to A
, which we refer to as the
open modality
for
\phi
; we
will leave both the
\lambda
-abstraction and application implicit in our
notation. Likewise, given an element
A:\phi\Rightarrow{\mathbb{U}}
we will write

\phi\Rightarrow{A}
for the
implicit
dependent product of
A
; this is
the
dependent open modality
.
5
5

5

The implicitness is only a
matter of convenient notation; it plays no mathematical role.

{defi}

[Closed modality]
We will write
\phi\bullet{A}
for the following quotient inductive type,
which we shall call the
closed modality
associated to
\phi
:

\mathbf{quotient}\ \mathbf{data}\ \phi\bullet{A}:\mathbb{U}\ \mathbf{where}

\eta_{\phi\bullet}:A\to\phi\bullet{A}

\star:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\_:\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\_:\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\_:\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\_:\phi$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\to{\phi\bullet{A}}

\_:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\_:\phi,u:\phi\bullet{A}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\_:\phi,u:\phi\bullet{A}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\_:\phi,u:\phi\bullet{A}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\_:\phi,u:\phi\bullet{A}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\to u=\star

(We use curly braces to indicate
implicit
arguments.)
Put another way,
\phi\bullet{A}
is the quotient of the coproduct
\phi+A

under the
\mathbb{P}
-valued equivalence relation
u\sim v\Longleftrightarrow\phi\lor\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u=v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u=v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u=v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u=v$}\mathclose{}{)}}
.
The elimination rule for
\phi\bullet{A}
is thus a case statement with a side condition:

x:\phi\bullet{A}\vdash Cx:\mathbb{U}
x:A\vdash fx:C\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\eta x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\eta x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\eta x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\eta x$}\mathclose{}{)}}
\_:\phi\vdash g:C\star
{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}x:A,\_:\phi\vdash fx=g:C\star}
u:\phi\bullet{A}
\mathbf{case}\ u\ \mathbf{of}\ \eta_{\phi\bullet}x\hookrightarrow fx\mid\star\hookrightarrow g:Cu

Note that we have only assumed an elimination rule for motives valued in
\mathbb{U}
.

Remark 8

(Effectivity)
.

Any equivalence relation valued in
\mathbb{P}
is effective, as
\mathbb{P}

satisfies propositional extensionality; thus the quotient in

Section

4.1
is effective. The only subtlety worth noting
is that although the equivalence relation
\phi\lor\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u=v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u=v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u=v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u=v$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is by
definition a pushout in
\mathbb{P}
of the projections
\phi\leftarrow\phi\land\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u=v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u=v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u=v$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u=v$}\mathclose{}{)}}\rightarrow u=v
, this pushout
need not
be preserved by
the inclusion
\mathbb{P}
\mathbb{U}
; an example of this behavior in semantics is that
the union of two regular subobjects need not be regular.

Fact 9
.

A type
A:\mathbb{U}
is
\phi
-transparent if and only if it is modal for the open
modality
\phi\Rightarrow{-}
; the type
A:\mathbb{U}
is
\phi
-sealed if and only if it
is modal for the closed modality
\phi\bullet{-}
.

In light of
9
, it is instructive to point out that the
elimination form for the closed modality

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x\leftarrow u;fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x\leftarrow u;fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x\leftarrow u;fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x\leftarrow u;fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}
can be implemented by

\mathbf{case}\ u\ \mathbf{of}\ \eta_{\phi\bullet}x\hookrightarrow fx\mid\star\hookrightarrow\star
.

Extension types

Given a type
A
, a proposition
\phi
and an element
a:\phi\Rightarrow{A}
we
will write
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\phi\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\phi\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phi\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phi\hookrightarrow a$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
for the subtype

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle x:A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\phi\Rightarrow{x=a}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle x:A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\phi\Rightarrow{x=a}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x:A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phi\Rightarrow{x=a}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x:A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phi\Rightarrow{x=a}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\subseteq A
. We will write

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\phi\hookrightarrow a,\psi\hookrightarrow b,\ldots$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\phi\hookrightarrow a,\psi\hookrightarrow b,\ldots$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phi\hookrightarrow a,\psi\hookrightarrow b,\ldots$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phi\hookrightarrow a,\psi\hookrightarrow b,\ldots$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
for the
extension type
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\phi\lor\psi\lor\ldots\hookrightarrow\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\phi\hookrightarrow a,\psi\hookrightarrow b,\ldots$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\phi\hookrightarrow a,\psi\hookrightarrow b,\ldots$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phi\hookrightarrow a,\psi\hookrightarrow b,\ldots$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phi\hookrightarrow a,\psi\hookrightarrow b,\ldots$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\phi\lor\psi\lor\ldots\hookrightarrow\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\phi\hookrightarrow a,\psi\hookrightarrow b,\ldots$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\phi\hookrightarrow a,\psi\hookrightarrow b,\ldots$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phi\hookrightarrow a,\psi\hookrightarrow b,\ldots$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phi\hookrightarrow a,\psi\hookrightarrow b,\ldots$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phi\lor\psi\lor\ldots\hookrightarrow\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\phi\hookrightarrow a,\psi\hookrightarrow b,\ldots$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\phi\hookrightarrow a,\psi\hookrightarrow b,\ldots$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phi\hookrightarrow a,\psi\hookrightarrow b,\ldots$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phi\hookrightarrow a,\psi\hookrightarrow b,\ldots$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phi\lor\psi\lor\ldots\hookrightarrow\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\phi\hookrightarrow a,\psi\hookrightarrow b,\ldots$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\phi\hookrightarrow a,\psi\hookrightarrow b,\ldots$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\phi\hookrightarrow a,\psi\hookrightarrow b,\ldots$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\phi\hookrightarrow a,\psi\hookrightarrow b,\ldots$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
when
\phi,\psi
are disjoint.

Remark 10

(LRAT axiomatizes the parametricity translation)
.

In the synthetic setting, to each type
A
we may associate the span

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\Rightarrow{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\Rightarrow{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\Rightarrow{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\Rightarrow{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\longleftarrow{A}\longrightarrow\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\Rightarrow{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\Rightarrow{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\Rightarrow{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\Rightarrow{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
given by the units of the open
modalities for
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}
; this should be thought of as the span that
A
is
taken to in the binary parametricity translation. Indeed, given
u_{\textsc{l}}:\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\Rightarrow{A}
and
u_{\textsc{r}}:\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\Rightarrow{A}
the subtype
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow u_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow u_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow u_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow u_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow u_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow u_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle A$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow u_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow u_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
can be
thought of as the type of proofs that
u_{\textsc{l}}
and
u_{\textsc{r}}
are related in the
correspondence.
It is in this sense that LRAT axiomatizes the parametricity translation.

A final axiom of the synthetic relational
extension of
iGDTT
is the
refinement type
.
6
6

6

In prior presentations of synthetic logical relations
[
SH21
,
SA21
]
,
the role played here by the refinement type connective (first axiomatized by Sterling and Harper
[
SH22
]
) was instead played by the so-called

realignment
axiom. The two are
interderivable
[
GSS22
, §5]
, but the refinement type connective
is both more direct and provides stronger geometrical intuition.

Given a type

A:\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{\mathbb{U}}
and a family of
\boldsymbol{\Phi}
-sealed types

B:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\mathbb{U}
, we may consider

\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum_{x:\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{A}}}{\textstyle\sum_{x:\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{A}}}{\scriptstyle\sum_{x:\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{A}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum_{x:\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{A}}}Bx
. The
refinement type
is a new code for
this dependent sum, written
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow x:A\mid Bx$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow x:A\mid Bx$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow x:A\mid Bx$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow x:A\mid Bx$}\mathclose{}{]}}
, that
enjoys the following
additional
principle of type equality: the
refinement type becomes equal to
A
in the presence of
\_:\boldsymbol{\Phi}
.
In other words, the refinement type is governed by the interface presented in
Fig.

1
.
We have not assumed that any proposition
\phi:\mathbb{P}
besides
\boldsymbol{\Phi}

is equipped with a refinement type connective.

formation

A:\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{A}
B:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\mathbb{U}
\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{\forall x:A.\ {Bx\cong 1}}
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow x:A\mid Bx$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow x:A\mid Bx$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow x:A\mid Bx$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow x:A\mid Bx$}\mathclose{}{]}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{U}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow A$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{U}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow A$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{U}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow A$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{U}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow A$}\mathclose{}{\}}}

{nota}

[Copattern notation]
We will often define elements of
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow x:A\mid Bx$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow x:A\mid Bx$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow x:A\mid Bx$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow x:A\mid Bx$}\mathclose{}{]}}
using
Agda-style “copattern” notation; for instance, the pair of declarations on
the left is meant to denote the single declaration on the right:

\begin{bmatrix}[l]{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}\hookrightarrow{u\triangleq a}\\
\underline{u}\triangleq b\end{bmatrix}\rightsquigarrow\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u\triangleq\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow a\mid b$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow a\mid b$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow a\mid b$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow a\mid b$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u\triangleq\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow a\mid b$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow a\mid b$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow a\mid b$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow a\mid b$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u\triangleq\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow a\mid b$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow a\mid b$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow a\mid b$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow a\mid b$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u\triangleq\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow a\mid b$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow a\mid b$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow a\mid b$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow a\mid b$}\mathclose{}{]}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

4.2.
The state monad in
iGDTT
{}^{\mathsf{ref}}_{\textsc{lrat}}

In
Section

3.1
, we extended
iGDTT
with general
reference types and a state monad
\mathsf{T}
. In this section, we add to the
axiomatization of
iGDTT
{}^{\mathsf{ref}}_{\textsc{lrat}}
constructs governing the weak bisimulation of
stateful computations. The following two rules make it possible to exhibit a
correspondence between two computations that take different numbers of steps:

\mathsf{step}_{\textsc{l}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{step},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{step},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{step},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{step},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}

\mathsf{step}_{\textsc{r}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{step}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{step}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{step}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{step}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}

{exa}

With the above in hand, it is possible exhibit a correspondence between two
computations that access the memory different numbers of times. Let

r:\mathsf{ref}\,{\mathbb{Z}}
and consider the programs
M_{\textsc{l}},M_{\textsc{r}}
defined
below:

M_{\textsc{l}}\triangleq x\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r;\mathsf{set}_{\mathbb{Z}}\,r\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x+1$}\mathclose{}{)}};y\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r;\mathsf{set}_{\mathbb{Z}}\,r\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle y+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle y+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle y+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle y+1$}\mathclose{}{)}};\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}

M_{\textsc{r}}\triangleq x\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r;\mathsf{set}_{\mathbb{Z}}\,r\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x+2$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x+2$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x+2$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x+2$}\mathclose{}{)}};\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}

We may define a correspondence
\mathcal{M}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
as
follows:

\mathcal{M}\triangleq x\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r;\mathsf{step}_{\textsc{l}};\mathsf{set}_{\mathbb{Z}}\,r\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x+2$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x+2$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x+2$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x+2$}\mathclose{}{)}};\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}

While
\mathcal{M}
is evidently of type
\mathsf{T}\,{1}
, it remains to argue that
it is equal to
M_{\textsc{l}}
(resp.
M_{\textsc{r}}
) under the assumption
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}

(resp.
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}
). Both claims follow from equational reasoning; assuming

\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}
we compute:

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{l}}

\displaystyle=x\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r;{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathsf{set}_{\mathbb{Z}}\,r\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x+1$}\mathclose{}{)}};y\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r;}\,\mathsf{set}_{\mathbb{Z}}\,r\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}y}+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}y}+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}y}+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}y}+1$}\mathclose{}{)}};\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\displaystyle=x\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r;\mathsf{step};{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathsf{set}_{\mathbb{Z}}\,r\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x+1$}\mathclose{}{)}};\mathsf{set}_{\mathbb{Z}}\,r\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x+1+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x+1+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x+1+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x+1+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}};\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\displaystyle=x\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r;\mathsf{step};\mathsf{set}_{\mathbb{Z}}\,r\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}x+1+1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}x+1+1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}x+1+1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}x+1+1}$}\mathclose{}{)}};\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\displaystyle=x\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r;{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathsf{step}};\mathsf{set}_{\mathbb{Z}}\,r\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x+2$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x+2$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x+2$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x+2$}\mathclose{}{)}};\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\displaystyle=x\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r;\mathsf{step}_{\textsc{l}};\mathsf{set}_{\mathbb{Z}}\,r\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x+2$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x+2$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x+2$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x+2$}\mathclose{}{)}};\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{M}

Likewise, assuming
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}
we have the following:

\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{r}}=x\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r;\mathsf{set}_{\mathbb{Z}}\,r\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x+2$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x+2$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x+2$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x+2$}\mathclose{}{)}};\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}=x\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r;\mathsf{step}_{\textsc{l}};\mathsf{set}_{\mathbb{Z}}\,r\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x+2$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x+2$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x+2$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x+2$}\mathclose{}{)}};\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle$}\mathclose{}{)}}=\mathcal{M}

4.3.
Case study: local references and closures

In this section we consider a synthetic version of an example from Birkedal,
Støvring, and Thamsborg
[
BST10
, §6.3]

concerning a correspondence between two imperative counter modules constructed
using a local reference and two closures. Whereas
op. cit
.
needed to work inside
the model, the combination of dependent types and synthetic relational
constructs enables us to work directly in the type theory. Consider the
following two implementations of an imperative counter module using local
references, one of which counts up and the other counts down:

M_{\textsc{l}},M_{\textsc{r}}:\mathsf{T}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

M_{\textsc{l}}\triangleq\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle r\leftarrow\mathsf{new}_{\mathbb{Z}}\,0;\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{incr}\,r,\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{incr}\,r,\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{incr}\,r,\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{incr}\,r,\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle r\leftarrow\mathsf{new}_{\mathbb{Z}}\,0;\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{incr}\,r,\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{incr}\,r,\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{incr}\,r,\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{incr}\,r,\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle M_{\textsc{r}}\triangleq\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle r\leftarrow\mathsf{new}_{\mathbb{Z}}\,0;\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{decr}\,r,x\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r;\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{decr}\,r,x\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r;\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{decr}\,r,x\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r;\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{decr}\,r,x\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r;\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle r\leftarrow\mathsf{new}_{\mathbb{Z}}\,0;\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{decr}\,r,x\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r;\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{decr}\,r,x\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r;\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{decr}\,r,x\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r;\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{decr}\,r,x\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r;\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle \mathsf{incr},\mathsf{decr}:\mathsf{ref}\,{\mathbb{Z}}\to\mathsf{T}\,{1}

\mathsf{incr}\,r\triangleq x\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r;\mathsf{set}_{\mathbb{Z}}\,r\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\mathsf{decr}\,r\triangleq x\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r;\mathsf{set}_{\mathbb{Z}}\,r\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x-1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x-1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x-1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x-1$}\mathclose{}{)}}

We wish to construct a correspondence between
M_{\textsc{l}}
and
M_{\textsc{r}}
,
i.e
.
an
element

\mathcal{M}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{T}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{T}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
. To do so, we define a correspondence on

\mathbb{Z}
describing the heap invariant using a refinement type:

\mathcal{Z}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{U}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{Z}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{U}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{Z}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{U}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{Z}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{U}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{Z}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}

\mathcal{Z}\triangleq\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow x:\mathbb{Z}\mid\boldsymbol{\Phi}\bullet{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle i_{\textsc{l}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{Z}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{Z}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{Z}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{Z}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow x$}\mathclose{}{\}}},i_{\textsc{r}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{Z}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{Z}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{Z}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow x$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{Z}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow
