Exegesis 11
.

It is important to understand why the invariant
\mathcal{Z}
is defined the way it is.

(1)

First we attach an element
x:\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}
, which is in
particular a pair of a “left” element
x_{\textsc{l}}:\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\Rightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}
and a “right” element

x_{\textsc{r}}:\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\Rightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}
with no conditions whatsoever.

(2)

We wish to assert that
x_{\textsc{r}}
is the negation of
x_{\textsc{l}}
; but this
is not type correct, because these partial integers lie in disjoint
worlds.

(3)

Instead we will glue onto the partial integer
x=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow x_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow x_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow x_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow x_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow x_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow x_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow x_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow x_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{]}}
a pair of
total integers
i_{\textsc{l}},i_{\textsc{r}}:\mathbb{Z}
restricting under

\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}
to
x_{\textsc{l}},x_{\textsc{r}}
such that
i_{\textsc{l}}=-i_{\textsc{r}}
.

(4)

It is necessary to hide the attachment of
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle i_{\textsc{l}},i_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle i_{\textsc{l}},i_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle i_{\textsc{l}},i_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i_{\textsc{l}},i_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
to

x
underneath the closed modality
\boldsymbol{\Phi}\bullet{-}
, since otherwise

\mathcal{Z}
would restrict to strictly more than just
\mathbb{Z}
under
\boldsymbol{\Phi}
. Without the closed modality, the formation rule for the refinement type employed here would not apply.

With the invariant
\mathcal{Z}
in hand, we will construct a
new

counter implementation
\mathcal{M}
that allocates an element of

\mathcal{Z}
; this new counter implementation should restrict “on the left”
to
M_{\textsc{l}}
and “on the right” to
M_{\textsc{r}}
. We divide the construction into
three main subroutines
\mathsf{init}_{\mathcal{M}}
,

\mathsf{tick}_{\mathcal{M}}
, and
\mathsf{read}_{\mathcal{M}}
:

\mathcal{M}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{T}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{r}},$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{T}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{r}},$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{r}},$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{T}\,{1}\times\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{r}},$}\mathclose{}{\}}}

\mathcal{M}\triangleq\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle r\leftarrow\mathsf{new}_{\mathcal{Z}}\,\mathsf{init}_{\mathcal{M}};\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{tick}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r,\mathsf{read}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{tick}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r,\mathsf{read}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{tick}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r,\mathsf{read}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{tick}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r,\mathsf{read}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle r\leftarrow\mathsf{new}_{\mathcal{Z}}\,\mathsf{init}_{\mathcal{M}};\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{tick}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r,\mathsf{read}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{tick}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r,\mathsf{read}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{tick}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r,\mathsf{read}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{tick}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r,\mathsf{read}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle r\leftarrow\mathsf{new}_{\mathcal{Z}}\,\mathsf{init}_{\mathcal{M}};\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{tick}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r,\mathsf{read}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{tick}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r,\mathsf{read}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{tick}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r,\mathsf{read}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{tick}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r,\mathsf{read}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle r\leftarrow\mathsf{new}_{\mathcal{Z}}\,\mathsf{init}_{\mathcal{M}};\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{tick}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r,\mathsf{read}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{tick}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r,\mathsf{read}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{tick}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r,\mathsf{read}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{tick}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r,\mathsf{read}_{\mathcal{M}}\,r$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

For the initialization, we simply construct an element of
\mathcal{Z}
witnessing
0=-0
.

\mathsf{init}_{\mathcal{M}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow 0$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathcal{Z}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow 0$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{Z}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow 0$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{Z}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow 0$}\mathclose{}{\}}}

{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}\hookrightarrow{\mathsf{init}_{\mathcal{M}}\triangleq 0}

\underline{\mathsf{init}_{\mathcal{M}}}\triangleq\eta_{\boldsymbol{\Phi}\bullet}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle 0,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle 0,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 0,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 0,0$}\mathclose{}{)}}

Next we describe the operation that ticks the counter, factoring through an
auxiliary operation
\mathsf{upd}_{\mathcal{M}}
that witnesses the
preservation of the
\mathcal{Z}
correspondence by the change to the reference
cell.

\mathsf{tick}_{\mathcal{M}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{ref}\,{\mathcal{Z}}\to\mathsf{T}\,{1}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{incr},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{decr}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{ref}\,{\mathcal{Z}}\to\mathsf{T}\,{1}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{incr},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{decr}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{ref}\,{\mathcal{Z}}\to\mathsf{T}\,{1}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{incr},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{decr}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{ref}\,{\mathcal{Z}}\to\mathsf{T}\,{1}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{incr},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{decr}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}

\mathsf{tick}_{\mathcal{M}}r\triangleq x\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\mathcal{Z}}r;\mathsf{set}_{\mathcal{Z}}\,r\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{upd}_{\mathcal{M}}x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{upd}_{\mathcal{M}}x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{upd}_{\mathcal{M}}x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{upd}_{\mathcal{M}}x$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\mathsf{upd}_{\mathcal{M}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Z}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\lambda x.x+1,\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\lambda x.x-1$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Z}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\lambda x.x+1,\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\lambda x.x-1$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Z}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\lambda x.x+1,\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\lambda x.x-1$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Z}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\lambda x.x+1,\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\lambda x.x-1$}\mathclose{}{\}}}

{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}}\hookrightarrow{\mathsf{upd}_{\mathcal{M}}x\triangleq x+1}

{\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}}\hookrightarrow{\mathsf{upd}_{\mathcal{M}}x\triangleq x-1}

\underline{\mathsf{upd}_{\mathcal{M}}x}\triangleq\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle i_{\textsc{l}},i_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle i_{\textsc{l}},i_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle i_{\textsc{l}},i_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i_{\textsc{l}},i_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\leftarrow\underline{x};\eta_{\boldsymbol{\Phi}\bullet}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle i_{\textsc{l}}+1,i_{\textsc{r}}-1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle i_{\textsc{l}}+1,i_{\textsc{r}}-1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle i_{\textsc{l}}+1,i_{\textsc{r}}-1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i_{\textsc{l}}+1,i_{\textsc{r}}-1$}\mathclose{}{)}}

In the definition of
\underline{\mathsf{upd}_{\mathcal{M}}x}
, we have used
the Kleisli extension for the closed modality
\boldsymbol{\Phi}\bullet{-}
. The
well-typedness of our definition follows from the fact that
i_{\textsc{l}}=-i_{\textsc{r}}

implies
i_{\textsc{l}}+1=-\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle i_{\textsc{r}}-1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle i_{\textsc{r}}-1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle i_{\textsc{r}}-1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i_{\textsc{r}}-1$}\mathclose{}{)}}
.
Finally we
construct a function to read the contents of the local state, factoring through
a function
\mathsf{tidy}_{\mathcal{M}}
that verifies the correspondence
between
x
and
-x
in the output:

\mathsf{read}_{\mathcal{M}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{ref}\,{\mathcal{Z}}\to\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\lambda r.\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r,\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\lambda r.x\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r;\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{ref}\,{\mathcal{Z}}\to\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\lambda r.\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r,\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\lambda r.x\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r;\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{ref}\,{\mathcal{Z}}\to\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\lambda r.\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r,\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\lambda r.x\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r;\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{ref}\,{\mathcal{Z}}\to\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\lambda r.\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r,\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\lambda r.x\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r;\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-x$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}

\mathsf{read}_{\mathcal{M}}r\triangleq x\leftarrow\mathsf{get}_{\mathcal{Z}}r;\mathsf{ret}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{tidy}_{\mathcal{M}}x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{tidy}_{\mathcal{M}}x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{tidy}_{\mathcal{M}}x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{tidy}_{\mathcal{M}}x$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\mathsf{tidy}_{\mathcal{M}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}\to\mathbb{Z}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\lambda x.x,\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\lambda x.{-x}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathcal{Z}\to\mathbb{Z}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\lambda x.x,\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\lambda x.{-x}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathcal{Z}\to\mathbb{Z}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\lambda x.x,\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\lambda x.{-x}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathcal{Z}\to\mathbb{Z}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\lambda x.x,\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\lambda x.{-x}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}

\mathsf{tidy}_{\mathcal{M}}x\triangleq

\mathbf{case}\ \underline{x}\ \mathbf{of}

\eta_{\boldsymbol{\Phi}\bullet}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle i_{\textsc{l}},i_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle i_{\textsc{l}},i_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle i_{\textsc{l}},i_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle i_{\textsc{l}},i_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\hookrightarrow i_{\textsc{l}}

\star\hookrightarrow\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow x,\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow-x$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow x,\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow-x$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow x,\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow-x$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow x,\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow-x$}\mathclose{}{]}}

Bisimulation of denotations

By interpreting the correspondence
\mathcal{M}
in our semantic model (see

Section

5
), it will follow that the results of initializing

M_{\textsc{l}},M_{\textsc{r}}
with any heap are
weakly bisimilar
,
i.e
.
equal up
to computation steps. In fact, this particular example exhibits a
strong

bisimulation.

4.4.
Case study: correspondences in abstract data types

We may adapt our previous example to exhibit a simulation between two
implementations of an abstract data type for imperative counters as in
Birkedal, Støvring, and Thamsborg
[
BST10
, §6.2]
. In particular, consider the
following abstract data type:

\mathsf{COUNTER}\triangleq\mathchoice{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\exists$}}}}}\displaylimits_{\alpha:\mathbb{U}}}{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\exists$}}}}}\displaylimits_{\alpha:\mathbb{U}}}{\scriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{321130}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\exists$}}}}}\displaylimits_{\alpha:\mathbb{U}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\exists$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{163842}{}{$\exists$}}}}}\displaylimits_{\alpha:\mathbb{U}}}\mathsf{T}\,\alpha\times\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\alpha\to\mathsf{T}\,{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\alpha\to\mathsf{T}\,{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\alpha\to\mathsf{T}\,{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha\to\mathsf{T}\,{1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\times\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\alpha\to\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\alpha\to\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\alpha\to\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha\to\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{Z}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}

We consider the following two implementations of
\mathsf{COUNTER}
:

M_{\textsc{l}}\triangleq\mathsf{pack}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{ref}\,{\mathbb{Z}},\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{new}_{\mathbb{Z}}\,0,\mathsf{incr},\lambda r.\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{new}_{\mathbb{Z}}\,0,\mathsf{incr},\lambda r.\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{ref}\,{\mathbb{Z}},\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{new}_{\mathbb{Z}}\,0,\mathsf{incr},\lambda r.\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{ref}\,{\mathbb{Z}},\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{new}_{\mathbb{Z}}\,0,\mathsf{incr},\lambda r.\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{new}_{\mathbb{Z}}\,0,\mathsf{incr},\lambda r.\mathsf{get}_{\mathbb{Z}}r$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{new}_{\mathbb{Z}}\,0,\mathsf{incr},\lambda M_{\textsc{r}}\triangleq\mathsf{pack}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{ref}\,{\mathbb{Z}},\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{new}_{\mathbb{Z}}\,0,\mathsf{decr},\lambda We may construct a correspondence between these two imperative counter
structures as follows, re-using the constructions of

Section

4.3
.

\mathcal{M}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{COUNTER}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{COUNTER}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{COUNTER}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{COUNTER}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow M_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}

\mathcal{M}=\mathsf{pack}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{ref}\,{\mathcal{Z}},\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{new}_{\mathcal{Z}}\,\mathsf{init}_{\mathcal{M}},\mathsf{tick}_{\mathcal{M}},\mathsf{read}_{\mathcal{M}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{new}_{\mathcal{Z}}\,\mathsf{init}_{\mathcal{M}},\mathsf{tick}_{\mathcal{M}},\mathsf{read}_{\mathcal{M}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{new}_{\mathcal{Z}}\,\mathsf{init}_{\mathcal{M}},\mathsf{tick}_{\mathcal{M}},\mathsf{read}_{\mathcal{M}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{new}_{\mathcal{Z}}\,\mathsf{init}_{\mathcal{M}},\mathsf{tick}_{\mathcal{M}},\mathsf{read}_{\mathcal{M}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{ref}\,{\mathcal{Z}},\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{new}_{\mathcal{Z}}\,\mathsf{init}_{\mathcal{M}},\mathsf{tick}_{\mathcal{M}},\mathsf{read}_{\mathcal{M}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{new}_{\mathcal{Z}}\,\mathsf{init}_{\mathcal{M}},\mathsf{tick}_{\mathcal{M}},\mathsf{read}_{\mathcal{M}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{new}_{\mathcal{Z}}\,\mathsf{init}_{\mathcal{M}},\mathsf{tick}_{\mathcal{M}},\mathsf{read}_{\mathcal{M}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{new}_{\mathcal{Z}}\,\mathsf{init}_{\mathcal{M}},\mathsf{tick}_{\mathcal{M}},\mathsf{read}_{\mathcal{M}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{ref}\,{\mathcal{Z}},\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{new}_{\mathcal{Z}}\,\mathsf{init}_{\mathcal{M}},\mathsf{tick}_{\mathcal{M}},\mathsf{read}_{\mathcal{M}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{new}_{\mathcal{Z}}\,\mathsf{init}_{\mathcal{M}},\mathsf{tick}_{\mathcal{M}},\mathsf{read}_{\mathcal{M}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{new}_{\mathcal{Z}}\,\mathsf Bisimulation of denotations

As in
Section

4.3
, by interpreting the
correspondence
\mathcal{M}
into the semantic model of
Section

5
we
may exhibit a weak (in fact, strong) bisimulation between the denotations of

M_{\textsc{l}}
and
M_{\textsc{r}}
when initialized with any heap.

5.
Semantic models of
iGDTT
and
iGDTT
{}^{\mathsf{ref}}_{\textsc{lrat}}

Thus far we have introduced a series of type theories—
iGDTT
,
iGDTT
ref
,
and
iGDTT
{}^{\mathsf{ref}}_{\textsc{lrat}}
—but we have not yet proven that these type theories are
consistent. In this section we justify all three theories by constructing
semantic models.

In
Section

5.1
we show to build a model of
iGDTT
based
combining realizability and the topos of trees. In

Section

5.2
, we show that presheaves internal to a
model of
iGDTT
also assemble into a model of
iGDTT
. Using this, we then
lift a base model to a model of
iGDTT
{}^{\mathsf{ref}}_{\textsc{lrat}}
in
Section

5.4

by taking presheaves on a carefully chosen category and constructing a more
sophisticated version of the possible-worlds model introduced in

Section

2.4
.
It proves most convenient to pass directly from a model of
iGDTT
to a model
of
iGDTT
{}^{\mathsf{ref}}_{\textsc{lrat}}
rather than constructing an intermediate model of

iGDTT
ref
. Of course, any model of
iGDTT
{}^{\mathsf{ref}}_{\textsc{lrat}}
is (by restriction) a
model of
iGDTT
ref
.

The construction of this sequence of models immediately yields the consistency
of all three type theories. The particular models, however, show more than
this. We use our model constructions to argue that a relation constructed in

iGDTT
{}^{\mathsf{ref}}_{\textsc{lrat}}
induces a weak bisimulation between the related programs in the
induced model of
iGDTT
ref
.

Remark 12
.

Unlike the rest of the paper, throughout this section we assume knowledge of
category theory. In particular, in addition to the basic concepts of category
theory we assume some familiarity with categorical realizability (assemblies,
modest sets,
etc
.
). We refer the reader to Van Oosten
[
vO08
]

or Streicher
[
Str17
]
for a thorough introduction.

5.1.
Constructing base models of
iGDTT

A concrete model of
iGDTT
can be constructed using a combination of
realizability and presheaves. Rather than belabor the already well-studied
interpretation of dependent type theory into well-adapted categories (
e.g
.
locally cartesian closed categories, topoi,
etc
.
)
[
Hof97
]
, we
describe and exhibit the relevant categorical structure needed to interpret the
nonstandard aspects of
iGDTT
: impredicative universes and guarded recursion.

Let
\mathscr{S}
be a realizability topos and let
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{O},\leq,\prec$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{O},\leq,\prec$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{O},\leq,\prec$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{O},\leq,\prec$}\mathclose{}{)}}
be a

separated intuitionistic well-founded poset
in
\mathscr{S}
,
i.e
.
a poset

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{O},\leq$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{O},\leq$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{O},\leq$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{O},\leq$}\mathclose{}{)}}
equipped with a transitive subrelation

{\prec}\subseteq{\leq}\subseteq\mathbb{O}\times\mathbb{O}
satisfying the
following additional conditions:

(1)

separation
: the object
\mathbb{O}
is an assembly and both
{\leq},{\prec}
are regular subobjects,

(2)

left compatibility
: if
u\leq v
and
v\prec w
then
u\prec w
,

(3)

right compatibility
: if
u\prec v
and
v\leq w
then
u\prec w
,

(4)

well-foundedness
: every element of
\mathbb{O}
is

\prec
-accessible, where the
\prec
-accessible elements are defined to be
the smallest subset
I\subseteq\mathbb{O}
such that if
v\in I
for all

v\prec u
, then
u\in I
.

Remark 13
.

In classical mathematics, the appropriate notion of well-founded poset
is considerably simpler as we tend to define
x\prec y\Leftrightarrow x\leq y\land x\not=y
. This simpler style of well-founded order is particularly
inappropriate for intuitionistic mathematics (
e.g
.
the mathematics of a
realizability topos), in which
\mathbb{O}
need not have
decidable equality. Our definition of intuitionistic well-founded posets is
inspired by Taylor’s analysis of intuitionistic ordinals
[
Tay96
]
.

Given a hierarchy of Grothendieck universes
\mathscr{U}_{i}
in
\mathbf{Set}
such that

\Gamma\mathbb{O}\in\mathscr{U}_{0}
, we conclude:

Theorem 14
.

The category of internal diagrams
7
7

7

We refer to Borceux
[
Bor94
, §8.1]
for discussion of internal diagrams.

\mathscr{E}=\mathchoice{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big[}{\big[}{\big[}{[}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{O}^{\circ},\mathscr{S}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big]}{\big]}{\big]}{]}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big[}{\big[}{\big[}{[}}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{O}^{\circ},\mathscr{S}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big]}{\big]}{\big]}{]}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big[}{\big[}{\big[}{[}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{O}^{\circ},\mathscr{S}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big]}{\big]}{\big]}{]}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big[}{\big[}{\big[}{[}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{O}^{\circ},\mathscr{S}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big]}{\big]}{\big]}{]}}}
is a
model of
iGDTT
in which:

(1)

the predicative universes
\mathbb{V}_{i}
are modelled by the
Hofmann–Streicher liftings
[
HS97
]
of the universes of
\mathscr{U}_{i}
-small assemblies in

\mathscr{S}
,

(2)

the impredicative universes
\mathbb{P},\mathbb{U}\in\mathbb{V}_{i}
is modelled
by the Hofmann–Streicher liftings of the universes of
\lnot\lnot
-closed
propositions and of modest sets in
\mathscr{S}
respectively,

(3)

the later modality
{\blacktriangleright}
is computed explicitly by the equation
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\blacktriangleright}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\blacktriangleright}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\blacktriangleright}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\blacktriangleright}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}u=\mathchoice{\textstyle\varprojlim_{\mathrlap{v\prec u}}}{\textstyle\varprojlim_{\mathrlap{v\prec u}}}{\scriptstyle\varprojlim_{\mathrlap{v\prec u}}}{\scriptscriptstyle\varprojlim_{\mathrlap{v\prec u}}}Av
.

Proof 5.1
.

The guarded recursive aspects follow from the general results of
Palombi and Sterling
[
PS23
]
; for the rest, it can be seen that the
Hofmann–Streicher lifting of a pair of universes preserves impredicativity
of the lower universe when
\mathbb{O}
is internal to the upper universe.
Our interpretation of
\mathbb{P}
is automatically univalent and satisfies
proof irrelevance, and the only subtle point is that it is closed under
equality. This follows because every type classified by
either
\mathbb{U}
or
\mathbb{V}
is
\lnot\lnot
-separated (since they are
assemblies), which means precisely that their equality predicates are

\lnot\lnot
-closed.

We describe the “standard” instantiation of
14
in
Section

5.1
below.

{exa}

[Standard model of
iGDTT
]
Let
\mathbf{Eff}
be the
effective topos

[
Hyl82
]
, namely the
realizability topos constructed from Kleene’s first algebra; there is a
modest intuitionistic well-founded poset
\omega
in
\mathbf{Eff}
given by the
natural numbers object of the topos under inequality and strict inequality.
Then
\mathchoice{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big[}{\big[}{\big[}{[}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\omega^{\circ},\mathbf{Eff}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big]}{\big]}{\big]}{]}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big[}{\big[}{\big[}{[}}\hbox{$\textstyle\omega^{\circ},\mathbf{Eff}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big]}{\big]}{\big]}{]}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big[}{\big[}{\big[}{[}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\omega^{\circ},\mathbf{Eff}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big]}{\big]}{\big]}{]}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big[}{\big[}{\big[}{[}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\omega^{\circ},\mathbf{Eff}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big]}{\big]}{\big]}{]}}}
is
model of
iGDTT
according to
14
.

From the non-emptiness of
\omega
it follows immediately that

\mathchoice{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big[}{\big[}{\big[}{[}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\omega^{\circ},\mathbf{Eff}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big]}{\big]}{\big]}{]}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big[}{\big[}{\big[}{[}}\hbox{$\textstyle\omega^{\circ},\mathbf{Eff}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big]}{\big]}{\big]}{]}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big[}{\big[}{\big[}{[}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\omega^{\circ},\mathbf{Eff}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big]}{\big]}{\big]}{]}}}{\mathopen{\mathchoice{\big[}{\big[}{\big[}{[}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\omega^{\circ},\mathbf{Eff}$}\mathclose{\mathchoice{\big]}{\big]}{\big]}{]}}}
is a non-trivial topos,
i.e
.
one in which the
initial object is not inhabited. Thus we obtain the following corollary:

Corollary 15
.

iGDTT
is consistent.

Remark 16

(Strong and weak completeness)
.

It is important that each
\mathbb{V}_{i}
be built from a subuniverse of small
assemblies

rather than of more general types in
\mathscr{S}
: the full internal subcategory spanned by modest sets
is strongly complete over assemblies, but only
weakly
complete over the rest of

\mathscr{S}

[
HRR90
]
. Weak completeness is insufficient to interpret

\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits
.

5.2.
The Hofmann–Streicher lifting of a model of
iGDTT

Let
\mathscr{E}
be an elementary topos model of
iGDTT

e.g
.
,
Section

5.1
; we
will show that for certain internal categories
\mathbb{C}
in
\mathscr{E}
, the topos of
internal diagrams
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{C},\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{C},\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{C},\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{C},\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}
is a model of
iGDTT
. To avoid
confusion, we will write things like
\mathbb{U},\mathbb{V}_{i}
for constructs of

\mathscr{E}
and
\overline{\mathbb{U}},\overline{\mathbb{V}_{i}}
for the corresponding constructs that
we will define in the category of internal diagrams
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{C},\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{C},\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{C},\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{C},\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}
.

Let
\mathbb{C}
be a category internal to
\mathbb{V}_{0}
,
i.e
.
a category object in

\mathscr{E}
whose object of objects and hom objects are classified by the universe

\mathbb{V}_{0}
. Then following
Hofmann and Streicher
[
HS97
]
we may lift each universe

\mathbb{X}\in\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{P},\mathbb{U},\mathbb{V}_{i}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{P},\mathbb{U},\mathbb{V}_{i}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{P},\mathbb{U},\mathbb{V}_{i}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{P},\mathbb{U},\mathbb{V}_{i}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
of
\mathscr{E}
to a universe

\overline{\mathbb{X}}
in
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{C},\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{C},\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{C},\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{C},\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}
, setting
\overline{\mathbb{X}}c=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle c\downarrow\mathbb{C},\mathbb{X}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle c\downarrow\mathbb{C},\mathbb{X}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle c\downarrow\mathbb{C},\mathbb{X}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle c\downarrow\mathbb{C},\mathbb{X}$}\mathclose{}{]}}
.
The only surprise is that
\overline{\mathbb{U}}
remains classified by
\overline{\mathbb{V}_{i}}
,
considering the fact that the coslice
c\downarrow\mathbb{C}
is large relative to

\mathbb{U}
. Nevertheless we have
\overline{\mathbb{U}}\in\overline{\mathbb{V}_{i}}
because
\mathbb{U}

is impredicative in
\mathbb{V}_{i}
; by an explicit computation, it follows that

\overline{\mathbb{U}}
can be encoded by an element of
\overline{\mathbb{V}_{i}}
using only

\mathchoice{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits}{\textstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{655368}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits}{\scriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{458758}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{321130}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits}{\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\vphantom{\sum}\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{0}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{327684}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{229379}{}{$\forall$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{163842}{}{$\forall$}}}}}\displaylimits}
,
\mathchoice{\textstyle\sum}{\textstyle\sum}{\scriptstyle\sum}{\scriptscriptstyle\sum}
, and
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle=$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle=$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle=$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle=$}\mathclose{}{)}}
in each fiber. The impredicativity of

\mathbb{U}
in
\mathbb{V}_{i}
likewise ensures that
\overline{\mathbb{U}}
is impredicative
over
\overline{\mathbb{V}_{i}}
.

Closure under the constructs of guarded dependent type theory then follows once
again from the general results of Palombi and
Sterling
[
PS23
]
concerning the pointwise lifting of
guarded recursion from the base into presheaves. It can also be seen that good
properties of the later modality are preserved by this lifting; if
\mathscr{E}
is

globally adequate
in the sense of
op. cit
.
and
\mathbb{C}
has an initial
object, then the
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{C},\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{C},\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{C},\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{C},\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}
is also globally adequate. Global
adequacy means that the global points of
{\blacktriangleright}{\mathbb{N}}
are the actual
natural numbers. Summarizing:

Lemma 17
.

Let
\mathscr{E}
be an elementary topos model of
iGDTT
and let
\mathbb{C}
be a category internal to
\mathbb{V}_{0}\in\mathscr{E}
.
The category of internal diagrams
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{C},\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{C},\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{C},\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{C},\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}
is a model of
iGDTT
in which:

(1)

all universes are modelled by the
Hofmann–Streicher liftings
[
HS97
]
of the corresponding universes in
\mathscr{E}
;

(2)

the later modality
{\blacktriangleright}
is computed pointwise,
i.e
.
we have

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\blacktriangleright}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\blacktriangleright}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\blacktriangleright}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\blacktriangleright}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}c={\blacktriangleright}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle Ac$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle Ac$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle Ac$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Ac$}\mathclose{}{)}}
.

Strict extension structures

In order to construct the store model in

5.4
, we will
require that the impredicative universe

also supports a

. While we require this property in only one concrete

Lemma 18

(Strictification)
.

Let
\Sigma
be a dominance such that each
\phi:\Sigma
is classified by

\mathbb{X}
; then there exists a universe
\mathbb{X}^{s}
equipped with a
strict extension structure for
\Sigma
such that
\mathbb{X}
and
\mathbb{X}^{s}

are
strongly equivalent
internal categories.

Proof 5.2
.

We set
\mathbb{X}^{s}
to be the
\Sigma
-partial element classifier

\mathbb{X}^{+}
itself. The strict algebra structure

is given by the multiplication

.

\mathbb{X}

.

We therefore combine
17
with

18
to obtain the following theorem.

Theorem 19
.

Let
\mathscr{E}
be an elementary topos model of
iGDTT
and let
\mathbb{C}
be a category internal to
\mathbb{V}_{0}\in\mathscr{E}
.
The category of internal diagrams
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{C},\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{C},\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{C},\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{C},\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}
is a model of
iGDTT
where:

(1)

the universes
\mathbb{P},\mathbb{V}_{i}
are modelled by Hofmann–Streicher
lifting;

(2)

the impredicative universe
\mathbb{U}\in\mathbb{V}_{i}
is modelled by a
universe strongly equivalent to the Hofmann–Streicher lifting of

\mathbb{U}:\mathscr{E}
, but equipped with a strict extension structure for

\mathbb{P}
;

(3)

the later modality
{\blacktriangleright}
is computed pointwise,
i.e
.
we have

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\blacktriangleright}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle{\blacktriangleright}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\blacktriangleright}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\blacktriangleright}{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}c={\blacktriangleright}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle Ac$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle Ac$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle Ac$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle Ac$}\mathclose{}{)}}
.

We will use this result twice: first to extend models of
iGDTT
with
constructs for relational reasoning and parametricity
(
Section

5.3
), and second to extend models of
iGDTT
with
higher-order store (
Section

5.4
). The culmination of this
process is a standard model for
iGDTT
{}^{\mathsf{ref}}_{\textsc{lrat}}
that modularly combines
realizability and presheaves.

5.3.
Logical relations as types in the span model of
iGDTT

Prior to the modeling
iGDTT
{}^{\mathsf{ref}}_{\textsc{lrat}}
, we expose the construction of the

span model
of
iGDTT
on top of a base model. This
intermediate model does not possess the necessary structure to interpret
references, but it does support the relational primitives of
iGDTT
{}^{\mathsf{ref}}_{\textsc{lrat}}
. We
will eventually use
iGDTT
itself as an internal language within this model in

Section

5.4
to build the store model.

5.3.1.
The span model

Given a topos model
\mathscr{E}
of
iGDTT
we may
take the topos
\mathbf{Span}\,\mathscr{E}
of spans in
\mathscr{E}
, which can be constructed as a
category of diagrams
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{C},\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{C},\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{C},\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{C},\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}
where
\mathbb{C}
is the generic
span
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{L}\leftarrow\mathsf{C}\rightarrow\mathsf{R}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{L}\leftarrow\mathsf{C}\rightarrow\mathsf{R}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{L}\leftarrow\mathsf{C}\rightarrow\mathsf{R}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{L}\leftarrow\mathsf{C}\rightarrow\mathsf{R}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
viewed as
an internal poset in
\mathbb{U}\in\mathscr{E}
. When
\mathscr{E}
is the category of
presheaves on a well-founded poset
\mathbb{O}
computed in a realizability
topos such as
\mathbf{Eff}
as in
Section

5.1
, we obtain from

19
a new model of
iGDTT
in
\mathbf{Span}\,\mathscr{E}
in
which all guarded constructs are computed pointwise.
The span model
\mathbf{Span}\,\mathscr{E}
contains propositions
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}
given by the
representables

\mathsf{y}_{\mathbb{C}^{\circ}}{\mathsf{L}},\mathsf{y}_{\mathbb{C}^{\circ}}{\mathsf{R}}

respectively. The span model is moreover closed under the
refinement

type connective
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow x:A\mid Bx$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow x:A\mid Bx$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow x:A\mid Bx$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow x:A\mid Bx$}\mathclose{}{]}}
where

\boldsymbol{\Phi}=\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}+\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}
is the (disjoint) union as usual; this follows as
the subterminals
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}
have pointwise decidable image in

\mathscr{E}

[
OP16
]
.

5.3.2.
Modal geometry of the span model

We may further unravel the open and closed modalities of
\mathbf{Span}\,\mathscr{E}
to
provide additional computational and geometric intuition. First we comment that
the propositions
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}
are concretely realized by the spans

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1_{\mathscr{E}}\leftarrow\emptyset_{\mathscr{E}}\rightarrow\emptyset_{\mathscr{E}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle 1_{\mathscr{E}}\leftarrow\emptyset_{\mathscr{E}}\rightarrow\emptyset_{\mathscr{E}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1_{\mathscr{E}}\leftarrow\emptyset_{\mathscr{E}}\rightarrow\emptyset_{\mathscr{E}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1_{\mathscr{E}}\leftarrow\emptyset_{\mathscr{E}}\rightarrow\emptyset_{\mathscr{E}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
,

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\emptyset_{\mathscr{E}}\leftarrow\emptyset_{\mathscr{E}}\rightarrow 1_{\mathscr{E}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\emptyset_{\mathscr{E}}\leftarrow\emptyset_{\mathscr{E}}\rightarrow 1_{\mathscr{E}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\emptyset_{\mathscr{E}}\leftarrow\emptyset_{\mathscr{E}}\rightarrow 1_{\mathscr{E}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\emptyset_{\mathscr{E}}\leftarrow\emptyset_{\mathscr{E}}\rightarrow 1_{\mathscr{E}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
,
and
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1_{\mathscr{E}}\leftarrow\emptyset_{\mathscr{E}}\rightarrow 1_{\mathscr{E}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle 1_{\mathscr{E}}\leftarrow\emptyset_{\mathscr{E}}\rightarrow 1_{\mathscr{E}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1_{\mathscr{E}}\leftarrow\emptyset_{\mathscr{E}}\rightarrow 1_{\mathscr{E}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1_{\mathscr{E}}\leftarrow\emptyset_{\mathscr{E}}\rightarrow 1_{\mathscr{E}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
respectively.

Characterization of open modalities

For any subterminal object
\phi
1_{\mathbf{Span}\,\mathscr{E}}
, the subcategory of
\mathbf{Span}\,\mathscr{E}
spanned by
\phi
-transparent
objects can be expressed as the slice
\mathbf{Span}\,\mathscr{E}\downarrow\phi
. But in the
case of the specific propositions that we have distinguished, more explicit
computations are available:

(1)

A
\boldsymbol{\Phi}
-transparent object is one that is isomorphic to a product span

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow X_{\textsc{l}}\times X_{\textsc{r}}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow X_{\textsc{l}}\times X_{\textsc{r}}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow X_{\textsc{l}}\times X_{\textsc{r}}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow X_{\textsc{l}}\times X_{\textsc{r}}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
.

(2)

A
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}
-transparent object is one that is isomorphic to a digram of
the form
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow X_{\textsc{l}}\rightarrow 1_{\mathscr{E}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow X_{\textsc{l}}\rightarrow 1_{\mathscr{E}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow X_{\textsc{l}}\rightarrow 1_{\mathscr{E}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow X_{\textsc{l}}\rightarrow 1_{\mathscr{E}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
.

(3)

A
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}
-transparent object is one that is isomorphic to a digram of
the form
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1_{\mathscr{E}}\leftarrow X_{\textsc{r}}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle 1_{\mathscr{E}}\leftarrow X_{\textsc{r}}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1_{\mathscr{E}}\leftarrow X_{\textsc{r}}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1_{\mathscr{E}}\leftarrow X_{\textsc{r}}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
.

Thus we compute the open modalities explicitly:

(1)

The open modality
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{-}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{-}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{-}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{-}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
takes a span

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow\tilde{X}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow\tilde{X}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow\tilde{X}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow\tilde{X}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}

to the span
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow X_{\textsc{l}}\times X_{\textsc{r}}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow X_{\textsc{l}}\times X_{\textsc{r}}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow X_{\textsc{l}}\times X_{\textsc{r}}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow X_{\textsc{l}}\times X_{\textsc{r}}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
.

(2)

The open modality
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\Rightarrow{-}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\Rightarrow{-}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\Rightarrow{-}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\Rightarrow{-}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
takes a span

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow\tilde{X}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow\tilde{X}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow\tilde{X}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow\tilde{X}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}

to the span
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow X_{\textsc{l}}\rightarrow 1_{\mathscr{E}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow X_{\textsc{l}}\rightarrow 1_{\mathscr{E}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow X_{\textsc{l}}\rightarrow 1_{\mathscr{E}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow X_{\textsc{l}}\rightarrow 1_{\mathscr{E}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
.

(3)

The open modality
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\Rightarrow{-}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\Rightarrow{-}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\Rightarrow{-}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\Rightarrow{-}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
takes a span

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow\tilde{X}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow\tilde{X}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow\tilde{X}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow\tilde{X}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}

to the span
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1_{\mathscr{E}}\leftarrow X_{\textsc{r}}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle 1_{\mathscr{E}}\leftarrow X_{\textsc{r}}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1_{\mathscr{E}}\leftarrow X_{\textsc{r}}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1_{\mathscr{E}}\leftarrow X_{\textsc{r}}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
.

Based on the above, it is easy to see (
e.g
.
) that
\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{A}
is
isomorphic to
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\Rightarrow{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\Rightarrow{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\Rightarrow{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\Rightarrow{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\times\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\Rightarrow{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\Rightarrow{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\Rightarrow{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\Rightarrow{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
.

Characterization of closed modality

Here we give an analogous discussion of the closed modality. A
\boldsymbol{\Phi}
-sealed
object is one that is isomorphic to a diagram of the form

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1_{\mathscr{E}}\leftarrow\tilde{X}\rightarrow 1_{\mathscr{E}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle 1_{\mathscr{E}}\leftarrow\tilde{X}\rightarrow 1_{\mathscr{E}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1_{\mathscr{E}}\leftarrow\tilde{X}\rightarrow 1_{\mathscr{E}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1_{\mathscr{E}}\leftarrow\tilde{X}\rightarrow 1_{\mathscr{E}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
. Then
the closed modality
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\bullet{-}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\bullet{-}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\bullet{-}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\bullet{-}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
takes a span
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow\tilde{X}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow\tilde{X}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow\tilde{X}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle X_{\textsc{l}}\leftarrow\tilde{X}\rightarrow X_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
to the span

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle 1_{\mathscr{E}}\leftarrow\tilde{X}\rightarrow 1_{\mathscr{E}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle 1_{\mathscr{E}}\leftarrow\tilde{X}\rightarrow 1_{\mathscr{E}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle 1_{\mathscr{E}}\leftarrow\tilde{X}\rightarrow 1_{\mathscr{E}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle 1_{\mathscr{E}}\leftarrow\tilde{X}\rightarrow 1_{\mathscr{E}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
.

5.3.3.
Synthetic weak bisimulation

In the span model of
iGDTT
, we may define a new type connective
\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{U}\to\mathbb{U}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{L}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{U}\to\mathbb{U}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{L}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{U}\to\mathbb{U}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{L}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{U}\to\mathbb{U}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{L}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
that expresses a weak bisimulation between two computations;
in other words, we will have
\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\,{A}=\mathsf{L}\,{A}}
and hence both
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\Rightarrow{\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\,{A}=\mathsf{L}\,{A}}

and
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\Rightarrow{\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\,{A}=\mathsf{L}\,{A}}
. Given

u_{\textsc{l}}:\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\Rightarrow{\mathsf{L}\,{A}}
and
u_{\textsc{r}}:\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\Rightarrow{\mathsf{L}\,{A}}
, an
element of
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\,{A}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow u_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow u_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\,{A}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow u_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow u_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\,{A}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow u_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow u_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\,{A}$}\mathrel{{|}}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow u_{\textsc{l}},\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow u_{\textsc{r}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}
will be a proof that
u_{\textsc{l}}
and
u_{\textsc{r}}

are weakly bisimilar. Because
A
itself can be any type (
e.g
.
any synthetic
correspondence), our definition of
\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\,{A}
can be seen to be an
adaptation of the lifting of a correspondence from
Møgelberg and Paviotti
[
MP16
]
.
We define
\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\,{A}
by solving a guarded recursive domain equation
in
\mathbb{U}
:

\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\,{A}\triangleq\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow u:\mathsf{L}\,{A}\mid\mathsf{WeaklyBisimilar}_{A}u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow u:\mathsf{L}\,{A}\mid\mathsf{WeaklyBisimilar}_{A}u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow u:\mathsf{L}\,{A}\mid\mathsf{WeaklyBisimilar}_{A}u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\hookrightarrow u:\mathsf{L}\,{A}\mid\mathsf{WeaklyBisimilar}_{A}u$}\mathclose{}{]}}

\mathbf{data}\ \mathsf{Done}_{A}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{\mathsf{L}\,{A}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{\mathsf{L}\,{A}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{\mathsf{L}\,{A}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{\mathsf{L}\,{A}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\mathbb{U}\ \mathbf{where}

\mathsf{stop}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle a:\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle a:\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle a:\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a:\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\mathsf{Done}_{A}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\eta a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\eta a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\eta a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\eta a$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\mathsf{waitR}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle a:\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{A},n:\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle a:\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{A},n:\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle a:\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{A},n:\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a:\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{A},n:\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\mathsf{Done}_{A}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\eta a,\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\delta^{n}\eta a$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\eta a,\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\delta^{n}\eta a$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\eta a,\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\delta^{n}\eta a$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\eta a,\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\delta^{n}\eta a$}\mathclose{}{]}}

\mathsf{waitL}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle a:\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{A},n:\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle a:\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{A},n:\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle a:\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{A},n:\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle a:\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{A},n:\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\mathsf{Done}_{A}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\delta^{n}\eta a,\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\eta a$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\delta^{n}\eta a,\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\eta a$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\delta^{n}\eta a,\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\eta a$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\delta^{n}\eta a,\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow\eta a$}\mathclose{}{]}}

\mathbf{quotient}\ \mathbf{data}\ \mathsf{WeaklyBisimilar}_{A}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{\mathsf{L}\,{A}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{\mathsf{L}\,{A}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{\mathsf{L}\,{A}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{\mathsf{L}\,{A}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\mathbb{U}\ \mathbf{where}

\mathsf{done}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle u:\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{\mathsf{L}\,{A}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle u:\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{\mathsf{L}\,{A}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u:\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{\mathsf{L}\,{A}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u:\boldsymbol{\Phi}\Rightarrow{\mathsf{L}\,{A}}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\to\mathsf{Done}_{A}u\to\mathsf{WeaklyBisimilar}_{A}u

\mathsf{step}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle u:{\blacktriangleright}\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\,{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle u:{\blacktriangleright}\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\,{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle u:{\blacktriangleright}\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\,{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u:{\blacktriangleright}\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\,{A}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\mathsf{WeaklyBisimilar}_{A}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\vartheta_{\mathsf{L}\,{A}}\,u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\vartheta_{\mathsf{L}\,{A}}\,u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\vartheta_{\mathsf{L}\,{A}}\,u$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\vartheta_{\mathsf{L}\,{A}}\,u$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\star:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\_:\boldsymbol{\Phi},u:\mathsf{L}\,{A}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\_:\boldsymbol{\Phi},u:\mathsf{L}\,{A}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\_:\boldsymbol{\Phi},u:\mathsf{L}\,{A}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\_:\boldsymbol{\Phi},u:\mathsf{L}\,{A}$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\to\mathsf{WeaklyBisimilar}_{A}u

\_:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\displaystyle\_:\boldsymbol{\Phi},u:\mathsf{L}\,{A},p:\mathsf{WeaklyBisimilar}_{A}u$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\textstyle\_:\boldsymbol{\Phi},u:\mathsf{L}\,{A},p:\mathsf{WeaklyBisimilar}_{A}u$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\_:\boldsymbol{\Phi},u:\mathsf{L}\,{A},p:\mathsf{WeaklyBisimilar}_{A}u$}\mathclose{}{\}}}{\mathopen{}{\{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\_:\boldsymbol{\Phi},u:\mathsf{L}\,{A},p:\mathsf{WeaklyBisimilar}_{A}u$}\mathclose{}{\}}}\to p=\star

We have ensured that
\mathsf{WeaklyBisimilar}_{A}
is valued in
\boldsymbol{\Phi}
-sealed types
by defining it as a
quotient inductive
definition; here again we recall
8
. Thus the use of the
refinement type in
\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\,{A}
is well-defined.
We note that
\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\,{A}
enjoys a similar interface to that of

\mathsf{L}\,{A}
; in particular, it inherits the structure of a guarded domain from
its components, meaning that it supports recursion:

{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}\hookrightarrow{\vartheta_{\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\,{A}}u\triangleq\vartheta_{\mathsf{L}\,{A}}\,u}

\underline{\vartheta_{\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\,{A}}u}\triangleq\mathsf{step}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{next}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle x\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle x\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}.\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{next}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle x\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle x\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}.\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{next}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle x\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle x\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}.\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{next}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle x\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle x\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}.\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}

Likewise, we may define a unit map
\widetilde{\eta}:A\to\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\,{A}
as follows:

{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}\hookrightarrow{\widetilde{\eta}\,a\triangleq\eta\,a}

\underline{\widetilde{\eta}\,a}\triangleq\mathsf{done}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{stop}\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{stop}\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{stop}\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{stop}\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}

In order to define the Kleisli extension for
\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\,{A}
we must
first define an operation that adds a step on the left or on the right; we
define the left-handed stepper
\delta_{\textsc{l}}
below, and treat
\delta_{\textsc{r}}
symmetrically.

\delta_{\textsc{l}}:\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\,{A}\to\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\,{A}

{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}\hookrightarrow{\delta_{\textsc{l}}u\triangleq\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\delta u,\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\delta u,\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\delta u,\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\delta u,\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}\hookrightarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}}

\underline{\delta_{\textsc{l}}u}\triangleq

\mathbf{case}\,\underline{u}\,\mathbf{of}

\mathsf{done}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{stop}\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{stop}\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{stop}\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{stop}\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{done}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{waitL}\,1\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{waitL}\,1\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{waitL}\,1\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{waitL}\,1\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\mathsf{done}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{waitL}\,a\,n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{waitL}\,a\,n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{waitL}\,a\,n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{waitL}\,a\,n$}\mathclose{}{)}}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{done}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{waitL}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{waitL}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{waitL}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{waitL}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n+1$}\mathclose{}{)}}\,a$}\mathclose{}{)}}

\mathsf{done}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{waitR}\,a\,n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{waitR}\,a\,n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{waitR}\,a\,n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{waitR}\,a\,n$}\mathclose{}{)}}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{step}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{next}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{waitR}_{?}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle The problem solved by strict extension above is the following: a type

A:\overline{\mathbb{U}}
at world
w
has extent only on the coslice

w\downarrow\mathbb{W}
, and yet to extend the world by a new cell we
must provide a type that has extent on all of
\mathbb{W}
. We use strict extension
to extend
A
to a global type in a way that agrees
strictly
with
A

after
w^{\prime}\geq w
.
We synthesize the preceding discussion into the following result:

Theorem 20
.

Let
\mathscr{E}
be an elementary topos model of
iGDTT
. The lifting of this
model to
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{W},\mathbf{Span}\,\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{W},\mathbf{Span}\,\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathbf{Span}\,\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathbf{Span}\,\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}
extends to a model of
iGDTT
{}^{\mathsf{ref}}_{\textsc{lrat}}
.

Proof 5.3
.

Applying
19
we obtain a model of
iGDTT
. The
relational constructs of
iGDTT
{}^{\mathsf{ref}}_{\textsc{lrat}}
(
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}
,
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}
,
etc
.
) are lifted
pointwise from
\mathbf{Span}\,\mathscr{E}
. The remaining constructs—those governing
state—are interpreted using the above definitions of
\mathsf{T}
,
\mathsf{new}
,

\mathsf{get}
, and
\mathsf{set}
.

Recalling that
iGDTT
ref
embeds into
iGDTT
{}^{\mathsf{ref}}_{\textsc{lrat}}
, we obtain the following
by the same reasoning as
15
:

Corollary 21
.

Both
iGDTT
ref
and
iGDTT
{}^{\mathsf{ref}}_{\textsc{lrat}}
are consistent.

Fix a closed term
M:\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{N}}
. Inspecting the interpretation of
M

within
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{W},\mathbf{Span}\,\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{W},\mathbf{Span}\,\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathbf{Span}\,\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{W},\mathbf{Span}\,\mathscr{E}$}\mathclose{}{]}}
, we see that it corresponds to a global
element of
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle M$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle M$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle M$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle M$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}:\hbox to37.89pt{\vbox to7.28pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\>\lower-0.33063pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

.

Given any semantic world
w
and heap
h:\mathbb{H}_{w}
, we therefore may run

t_{\emptyset}
to obtain an element
\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\,\mathbb{N}
within

\mathbf{Span}\,\mathscr{E}
. We present this using
iGDTT
as the internal language of

\mathbf{Span}\,\mathscr{E}
:

\mathsf{run}:\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle w:\mathbb{W}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle w:\mathbb{W}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle w:\mathbb{W}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle w:\mathbb{W}$}\mathclose{}{)}}\to\mathbb{H}_{w}\to\widetilde{\mathsf{L}}\,\mathbb{N}

\mathsf{run}_{w}\ h\ t=\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle w^{\prime},h^{\prime},n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle w^{\prime},h^{\prime},n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle w^{\prime},h^{\prime},n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle w^{\prime},h^{\prime},n$}\mathclose{}{)}}\leftarrow t_{\emptyset}\ w\ h;\eta\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle n$}\mathclose{}{)}}

Denoting the restriction of
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle M$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle M$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle M$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle M$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}
by
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}
(resp.
\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{r}}
) by

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle M$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle M$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle M$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle M$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}_{\textsc{l}}
(resp.
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle M$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle M$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle M$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle M$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}_{\textsc{r}}
), the preceding discussion now
substantiates the claim made in
Section

4.3
:

Theorem 22
.

Fix a closed term
M:\mathsf{T}\,{\mathbb{N}}
in
iGDTT
{}^{\mathsf{ref}}_{\textsc{lrat}}
. For any
w:\mathbb{W}

and a heap
h:\mathbb{H}_{w}
, there is an element of

\mathsf{WeaklyBisimilar}_{\mathbb{N}}{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}_{\textsc{l}}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{run}_{w}\ h\ \mathchoice{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\displaystyle M$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\textstyle M$}\mathclose{}{\rrbracket}}{\mathopen{}{\llbracket}\hbox{$\scriptstyle
